# Branch Dispositions — the repository fact

**Created by phase GOV-3 on 2026-09-04** (branch `agrimore/gov3-agrimore-skill-and-branch-model`,
from `main` = `c8f6f30`). This file exists because, until today, every decision about what should
happen to a branch in this repository lived only in one CTO session's private memory, and sessions
do not share memory. Four or more concurrent sessions have edited this checkout at once
(`agrimore-concurrency-incidents` in CTO memory: seven incidents, one materially false commit
message), always colliding on `firestore.rules` and `functions/src/index.ts`. A row here is the
only way a second session can discover that a phase is already claimed.

**This file is the authority on branch disposition. Session memory is not.** Operating rules:
`.claude/skills/agrimore/references/{merge,promote}.md`; validator:
`node scripts/governance/validate-branch-dispositions.mjs` (warnings only, exit 0 — read them).

## The rules

- **A branch absent from this file has NO disposition and MUST NOT be merged.** Absence is not
  neutral. Find out before merging.
- **Only the owner changes a disposition.** A session may ADD a row for a branch it just created
  and may move its OWN row `ACTIVE` → `MERGED_DEVELOP` in the bookkeeping commit after the merge.
  A session never changes another branch's `Status` cell — not even to correct it. Flag and ask.
- **CLAIM BEFORE YOU START — the row is a lock, not a receipt.** See the claim protocol.
- **Phase branches merge into `develop`, never into `main`.** `develop` is the integration branch
  (local end-to-end on the Firebase emulator suite). `staging` and `main` move only by
  fast-forward promotion with the owner's word in the same message, recorded in `## Promotions`.
  A merge commit or a direct commit on `staging`/`main` is a defect.
- **Nothing here authorises `firebase deploy`.** Promotion is a git event. Deploying to
  `agrimore-66a4e` (live, real users) is the owner's own action, always with explicit function
  names; the skill prints the command and stops.

## Claim protocol — read this before creating a branch

1. **Collision check, three sources, every time:** `git worktree list` · `git branch --list
   'agrimore/*'` · this file's `ACTIVE` rows, read for topic overlap (not just branch name). A
   claim row on an unmerged branch is invisible from `develop` — that is why all three are needed.
2. **Create the worktree from `main`** (or from `develop` once phases have landed there — the
   contract's `base` says which): `git worktree add ../Agrimore-<slug> -b agrimore/<id>-<slug> <base>`.
3. **The claim row is the FIRST commit on the branch.** `Status` = `ACTIVE`, `Why` names the files
   you expect to touch and the collision check you ran, `Merged into` = `N/A — pending`.
   Commit message: `docs(repo): claim phase <ID> — <slug>`. Verify with
   `git log -1 --format='%an <%ae> | %cn <%ce>'` (must be `Agrimore <agrimorein@gmail.com>`).
4. Keep the `Why` cell growing with findings; commit ledger edits immediately.
5. After the merge (inside the `develop` worktree): the bookkeeping commit sets your row to
   `MERGED_DEVELOP`, fills `SHA` with the branch tip and `Merged into` with the merge commit.

## Status tokens

| Token | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| `ENVIRONMENT` | One of `develop` · `staging` · `main`. Moves only per the fast-forward model; its `SHA` cell is informational (re-measure), never a lock. |
| `ACTIVE` | In flight, owned by a running phase. No disposition decided yet — expected. |
| `MERGED_DEVELOP` | Contained in `develop`. `Merged into` names the `--no-ff` merge commit on `develop`. Reaches `staging`/`main` only through a promotion recorded below. |
| `MERGED` | Legacy: contained in `main` from before the branch model existed (the tip sits on `main`'s own history). |
| `PRESERVED_REFERENCE` | Deliberately **NOT** merged; kept as input to a named future phase. **NEVER merge without a new owner decision.** |
| `SUPERSEDED_BY:<branch>` | Replaced by `<branch>`. Do not merge this branch. |
| `ARCHIVED_TAG:<tag>` | Content preserved as tag `<tag>`; the branch may be deleted. |

list`, `git merge-base --is-ancestor`), not from memory. `master` was renamed to `main` the same day
(OWNER_DECISION D-BRANCH); `develop` and `staging` were created at the same tip (re-level). The
remote `origin` (`SRIESWARAN01/Agrimore-Full-Project`, public) still has only `master` at `0ea1e53`,
64 commits behind — every push is the owner's action (the machine's GitHub credential is
`Edynox-hq`, which has no write access there).

Re-measured at base `f83c63f`, not carried from the audit: a grep for any stock write across `functions/src` returns **0** — `computeOrderPricing` (`orderPricing.ts`) validates `product.stock` and nothing anywhere writes it back; `updateStockAfterOrder` (`packages/agrimore_services/lib/orders/order_service.dart`) has **0** callers and is dead code that would be denied by the `products` rules anyway; and `createOrder.ts` has **0** occurrences of an item de-duplication or an `items.length` cap. Consequences, all live: stock never falls, an out-of-stock product stays purchasable forever, `soldCount` never increments, `onProductStockChanged` low-stock alerts can only ever fire from a manual seller edit and never from a sale, and a cart listing the same `productId` twice passes the per-item stock check twice (`items:[{p,5},{p,5}]` against `stock:5` is accepted). BLAST RADIUS ESTABLISHED BEFORE BUILDING (the lesson from FIX-1, which needed three late amendments): `computeOrderPricing` has TWO callers — `createOrder.ts:346` and `productCreditHold.ts:174` (`quoteOrderWithCredit`). De-duplication and the item cap belong in the shared module so the quote and the order cannot disagree (that shared-implementation guarantee is the reason `orderPricing.ts` exists — see its header), which means the credit-quote path changes too and `quoteOrderWithCredit` must be redeployed alongside `createOrder`. The stock DECREMENT, by contrast, belongs in `createOrder.ts` only: a quote must never reserve or consume stock. | `agrimore/fix4-payout-integrity` | `e0c829f` | `MERGED_DEVELOP` | 2026-09-05 | session FIX-4 | Phase FIX-4: seller-payout correctness (findings **N-7**, **N-8**, **N-26**, **N-28**). All four re-measured at base `55c80ad`, not carried from the audit. **N-7**: `functions/src/customer/sellerNotifications.ts` `calculateSellerPayout` performs a non-transactional check-then-act — a `where(orderId).where(sellerId).limit(1).get()`, an early return if non-empty, then a bare `.add()` with a random document id. v1 Firestore triggers are at-least-once and can be delivered concurrently, so two invocations both observe `empty` and both `add()`, paying the seller twice. `payEmployeeCommissionOnDelivery` in the sibling file gets this right by re-checking inside `runTransaction`; this one never did. **N-8**: line 144 fires only on the exact string `orderStatus === "delivered"`, while `employeeCommission.ts` accepts `delivered` AND `completed`, case-insensitively, and its own comment records that `apps/admin`'s bulk action writes the literal `'completed'`. An order closed that way pays the associate and silently never creates a `seller_payouts` document at all. **N-26**: the payout re-derives `sellerId` from the LIVE `products/{id}` document (line ~180) rather than from the order's own stored per-item `sellerId` — which `orderPricing.ts:327` does write. A product reassigned to another seller after the order pays the WRONG seller; a deleted product hits `if (!sellerId) continue;` and silently drops that item from the payout entirely. **N-28**: `categoryRates[firstCategory]` and `defaultRate` are used with no finite/positive/ceiling validation, so a bad admin entry yields `NaN` or a negative `netAmount`; `employeeCommission.ts` validates carefully via `resolveCommissionRate` and this file does not. Also observed while measuring, in scope as a side effect of the same rewrite: `settings/commission` is read once at the top for `defaultCommission` AND again inside the per-seller map for `categoryRates`, an N+1 read; and `defaultRate || 8` silently turns a configured rate of 0 into 8. SPLIT, DELIBERATE: three findings originally scoped here were moved OUT to a new phase **FIX-4B**, which is `BLOCKED` on the owner — **N-9** (no reversal of commission or seller payout on cancellation/refund), **N-29** (commission computed on `after.total`, which includes delivery charge and tax, so associates earn commission on tax), and **N-27** (the whole payout's rate taken from the FIRST item's category, so mixed-category orders get the wrong rate). Each changes how much real money real people receive and none has a defensible default; N-27 is a genuine bug and was still moved, because "this is wrong" and "I may unilaterally change what sellers are paid" are different claims. Files expected: `docs/active/BRANCH_DISPOSITIONS.md` (this row), `functions/src/customer/sellerNotifications.ts`, `functions/scripts/phase28_payout_integrity_test.js` (new), `.claude/skills/agrimore/references/security.md`. `must_not_touch`: `firestore.rules`, `storage.rules`, `apps/**`, `packages/**`, `functions/src/customer/createOrder.ts`, and `functions/src/customer/employeeCommission.ts` (N-29 lives there and belongs to FIX-4B). Invariants to preserve: `phase16d1_commission_test`, `phase16d2_attribution_test`, `phase15_order_integrity_test`, `phase25`, `phase27`. NOTE: `calculateSellerPayout` is exported from `sellerNotifications.ts`; if the rewrite touches anything `notifySellerNewOrder` shares, that name joins the command and the row will say so. Built 2026-09-05 in 4 commits (`85d8e10` claim, `de02b2b` the rewrite, `bec229f` phase28 suite, `e0c829f` register). Baseline at `85d8e10`: failed=0. Final gate at `e0c829f`: failed=0. NEW SUITE `phase28_payout_integrity_test.js` 8/8 on a fresh emulator, driving the real compiled trigger as a v1 onUpdate (v1 `wrap` takes a Change and a context; calling it the v2 way would silently yield an empty context). REVERT-AND-WATCH, the strongest of the run — the reverted build did not merely fail, it EXECUTED all four findings: s2 `payouts=2` (the seller paid TWICE from two genuinely concurrent invocations), s3 `payouts=0` (a `completed` order pays nothing), s4 `seller=phase28-NEW-owner` (the WRONG seller paid after a product reassignment), s5 `payouts=0` (a deleted product dropped the line entirely), s6 `net=NaN` written into a payout document, s7 `net=-400` (a NEGATIVE payout). Controls s1 and s8 still PASSED, so the probe discriminates rather than going uniformly red. Restored: abort → porcelain 0, rebuild, 8/8 again. Scenario 2 fires both invocations CONCURRENTLY rather than sequentially: a sequential double-call is caught by almost any check and would prove very little, while at-least-once delivery means genuinely overlapping execution, which is what the deterministic id plus the transaction actually has to survive. Security lane 5/5 on fresh emulators — phase16d1_commission, phase16d2_attribution, phase15_order_integrity, phase27_stock_integrity, phase25_payment_single_consumption (the last two included to confirm FIX-3 and FIX-1 still hold under this change). SELF-CORRECTION, recorded because the commit message is what the ledger and the register quote: the WS1 message originally listed ``defaultRate || 8`` swallowing a configured 0 among things FIXED. It is not fixed — `resolvePayoutRate(0, 8)` returns 8, identical to `||` for that input — and it is deliberately PRESERVED, matching `employeeCommission.ts`'s documented rate-0 semantics (0 means unconfigured, not pay-nothing, because `commissionRate: 0` is the literal default for newly self-applied records). Caught by re-reading the committed code before writing the suite and amended `fcc1639` → `de02b2b` via `git commit --amend --only -m` BEFORE any evidence was taken. PHASE SPLIT, decided before claiming: **N-9** (no reversal of commission or seller payout on cancellation/refund), **N-29** (commission computed on `after.total`, which includes delivery and tax, so associates earn commission on tax) and **N-27** (the whole payout's rate taken from the FIRST item's category) were moved to a new `BLOCKED` phase **FIX-4B**. Each changes how much real money real people receive and none has a defensible default; N-27 is a genuine bug and was still moved, because "this is wrong" and "I may unilaterally change what sellers are paid" are different claims. The category-rate SELECTION RULE is therefore preserved verbatim here, and the product document is still read for `categoryId` only. Re-measured at base `6f0abd4`: `apps/delivery/lib/screens/orders/active_order_screen.dart:801` reads `deliveryVerificationCode` out of the order document **into the delivery partner's own client** and compares it locally, then `:868` calls `orderProvider.updateOrderStatus(id, 'delivered', …)` which writes the status directly. The party being authenticated is handed the answer, and the dialog can be skipped entirely — `orderFulfilmentCannotChangeProtectedFields()` denylists financial fields but NOT `orderStatus`/`status`, so the direct write is permitted by the rules. Chained with the payout triggers FIX-4 just made reliable, a delivery partner can self-certify delivery and cause a real seller payout and a real associate commission on goods never delivered. In scope: a new `confirmDelivery` callable that takes the order id and the code, compares server-side (never returning the code), and performs the transition; its export in `functions/src/index.ts`; `apps/delivery` calling it instead of comparing locally; and an emulator suite proving a wrong code is refused, a right code transitions exactly once, and a second call is idempotent. It would take effect the instant the rules deploy landed and would break EVERY delivery partner still running the currently-released `apps/delivery` build, which writes that status directly. That is precisely the 2026-08-31 failure mode, when a rules deploy left pre-1.0.7 clients unable to place orders (B2B_PHASE_SEQUENCING). **HONEST CONSEQUENCE: FIX-5 alone does not close N-5.** The direct write still works until FIX-5B lands, so a partner can still self-certify. What FIX-5 buys is that the correct path exists and can be adopted by a released build FIRST — the only safe order for a live app. Files expected: `docs/active/BRANCH_DISPOSITIONS.md` (this row), `functions/src/customer/confirmDelivery.ts` (new), `functions/src/index.ts` (one export), `apps/delivery/lib/screens/orders/active_order_screen.dart`, `apps/delivery/lib/providers/order_provider.dart`, `functions/scripts/phase29_delivery_confirmation_test.js` (new), `.claude/skills/agrimore/references/security.md`. `must_not_touch`: `firestore.rules` (FIX-5B owns it), `storage.rules`, `apps/marketplace/**`, `apps/seller/**`, `packages/**`. Final gate at `f76c8ef`, the last code commit: failed=0, `analyze:delivery` errors=0 warnings=3 (unchanged from base). NEW SUITE `phase29_delivery_confirmation_test.js` **12/12** on a fresh emulator, driving the real compiled v2 onCall as `wrapped({data, auth})`. REVERT-AND-WATCH, three separate probes, each a DELIVERY rather than a soft failure: (1) assignment check removed → 5/8 at the time, and s3 showed an UNASSIGNED partner holding the correct code reaching `status=delivered`, s8 showed an order with NO assigned partner deliverable by anyone, s4 showed right and wrong guesses becoming distinguishable (`identical=false`) — the oracle the check ordering exists to prevent; (2) `statusIsIn` narrowed to `orderStatus` only → 10/12, s10 returned `orderStatus=delivered status=delivered` on a SELLER-CANCELLED order and s11 re-delivered an already-delivered one with a second timeline entry; (3) `NOT_DELIVERABLE` block removed → 9/12, s9 delivered a cancelled order (`status=delivered`, `timeline=1`) and s12 became distinguishable. Restored after each: rebuild 0, 12/12, `git status --porcelain` empty. SECURITY LANE FOUND TWO DEFECTS IN THIS PHASE'S OWN NEW CODE and both were fixed before merge, registered as **N-5a** and **N-5b**: a cancelled order was deliverable (a real seller payout and associate commission on goods the customer is not receiving, on the very transition FIX-4 had just made reliable), and the status was read from `orderStatus` alone when `productCreditReversal.ts:50` and `firestore.rules`' `ownerOrderStatusChangeIsValid()` both treat `orderStatus` and the mirrored `status` as one signal for a reason written down in both — the customer cancel path writes only `orderStatus`, the seller/admin panel writes ONLY `status`. UIUX/FEEDBACK LANE, one finding, fixed: the verify button had no in-flight state, defensible when the check was a local read and a string compare, not defensible against a `minInstances: 0` callable that cold-starts — the button now disables, spins and reads "Verifying…", and Cancel disables with it. The two `failed-precondition` refusals mean different things to a partner at a door, so the callable tags them (`reason: "no_code"` / `"not_deliverable"`) and the client maps the tag rather than parsing prose; an absent tag falls back to the support wording, safe for either. SCOPE DEVIATIONS, both deliberate: `apps/delivery/lib/providers/order_provider.dart` was in `may_write` and was NOT touched — the callable replaced the provider call outright, so editing the provider would have been churn; and `apps/delivery/pubspec.yaml` + `pubspec.lock` were NOT in `may_write` and WERE touched (Amendment 1) — `cloud_functions` was a transitive dependency and calling `FirebaseFunctions` from app code requires a direct declaration. The lock diff is one line, `transitive` → `direct main`, same version and same sha256. `grep -c deliveryVerificationCode apps/delivery` = **0**. HONEST LIMIT, restated because it is the whole point of the split: this phase does NOT close N-5. The direct status write is still permitted by the rules, so until FIX-5B lands AND a released `apps/delivery` build uses this callable, a delivery partner can still self-certify. The busy state is compile-verified and reviewed, not exercised on a device — `apps/delivery` has no test suite and this phase built no APK. | `agrimore/fix9-money-hygiene` | `f18179b` | `MERGED_DEVELOP` | 2026-09-05 | session FIX-9 | Phase FIX-9: payment/money hygiene, 8 workstreams (findings **N-13**, **N-14**, **N-30**, **N-33**, **N-34**, **N-46**, **N-47**, **N-48**). Re-measured fresh at base `f83442b`. **WS1 (signing oracle)**: confirmed `payment.ts:173` and `wallet.ts:134` both persist `expectedSignature` (the correct HMAC-SHA256) into `payment_security_logs` on a mismatch. SEVERITY REFINED, not overstated: `firestore.rules:1488-1491` already gates this collection `allow read: if isAdmin()` — an ordinary client cannot read it back, so this is NOT the public oracle a bare reading of the finding might suggest. Still fixed, because 'admin-only' is not the same claim as 'safe to persist a reusable forgery credential' — an admin-account compromise, a future admin-dashboard rendering of this collection, or a Firestore backup would all leak it. **WS2 (notes spread)**: confirmed `notes: { userId: request.auth.uid, ...notes }` — client-supplied `notes.userId` overwrites the server's own value. **WS3 (tolerance + payable branch)**: confirmed both `verifiedAmount` and `payable` are RUPEE-denominated (payment.ts:214 converts Razorpay's paise back to rupees before storage), so the existing `> 1` (one full RUPEE) tolerance is not explained by any legitimate rounding step — `grandTotal` is rounded exactly ONCE in aggregate (`orderPricing.ts:377`), so true rounding drift between two independently-computed totals is on the order of a paisa, not a rupee. The contract flagged this as an owner decision ('may reject legitimate orders'); investigated instead of deferred, tightened to 0.02 (2 paise) — generous enough to absorb a single independent rounding step on each side, closing roughly 98% of the previous gap. Documented as a reasoned engineering choice, not a business policy call; the owner can adjust the one constant if real-world false-rejects appear. The `payable > 1` branch (below which NO Razorpay payment was required at all) tightened to the same `0.02` epsilon for consistency — this part was not a tolerance question, it was skipping payment collection entirely for residuals up to a full rupee. **WS4 (threshold validation + claim-first)**: confirmed `setLowStockThreshold` writes `threshold` with zero validation, and reads `userDoc` unconditionally via `Promise.all` even when the caller's own auth token already carries `admin: true` — the codebase's OWN established idiom (`createEmployeeByAdmin.ts`/`createSellerByAdmin.ts`'s `callerIsAdmin`) checks the claim FIRST and only falls back to a Firestore read when needed. **WS5 (order number)**: confirmed `generateOrderNumber()` uses `Math.random()` with no uniqueness constraint — `Date.now()` plus a 4-digit `Math.random()` tail is guessable and collision-prone under concurrent orders in the same millisecond. **WS6 (OTP compare)**: confirmed `verifyPhoneOTP.ts:145` uses a plain `!==` on the OTP hash — mirrors exactly the pattern FIX-5's `confirmDelivery.ts` already fixed with `crypto.timingSafeEqual`; reusing that established idiom, not inventing a new one. **WS7 (rounding residual)**: confirmed `orderPricing.ts:390-393` rounds EVERY seller's `discount`/`deliveryCharge`/`tax`/`total` independently with no reconciliation step, so `sum(perSeller[].total)` can drift from `grandTotal` by roughly `sellerCount × 0.5` paisa — a real, if small, per-seller-payout reconciliation gap. `createOrder.ts:580`'s comment shows the 'last seller absorbs residual' pattern ALREADY exists for `creditApplied` distribution; generalizing that same pattern to `orderPricing.ts`'s own per-seller split, for all four fields, not just `total`. **WS8 (response/error narrowing)**: confirmed `verifyRazorpayPayment` returns the FULL raw Razorpay `payment` object (potentially including email/contact/bank fields) to the calling client, and its catch block rethrows `error.message` verbatim — including, in `createRazorpayOrder`, a literal internal ops runbook line ('Run: firebase functions:config:set...') as a client-facing error. Narrowed the response to what a client actually needs and the error to a generic, client-safe message, matching feedback.md's own 'never render the raw provider message' principle already applied in FIX-5. Also confirmed and dropped the dead `transfers` field — destructured from client input in `createRazorpayOrder` and never referenced again anywhere in the function. Files expected: `docs/active/BRANCH_DISPOSITIONS.md` (this row), `functions/src/customer/payment.ts`, `functions/src/customer/wallet.ts`, `functions/src/customer/createOrder.ts`, `functions/src/customer/orderPricing.ts`, `functions/src/customer/inventory.ts`, `functions/src/common/verifyPhoneOTP.ts`, `functions/scripts/phase32_money_hygiene_test.js` (new), `.claude/skills/agrimore/references/security.md`. `must_not_touch`: `firestore.rules`, `storage.rules`, `apps/**`, `packages/**`. | `agrimore/fix10-notification-delivery` | `e6da3ba` | `MERGED_DEVELOP` | 2026-09-05 | session FIX-10 | Phase FIX-10: broadcast-notification audience gap and unpaginated user scan (findings **N-16**, **N-17**). RE-VERIFIED, and the finding is NARROWER and DIFFERENT in mechanism than the original scan's one-line description — recorded honestly rather than carried forward: the original text says '`packages/agrimore_services/lib/notifications/fcm_service.dart:128` and `apps/marketplace` `auth_provider.dart:117` write only `fcmToken` (singular)'. That is STALE. `fcm_service.dart:128-129` ALREADY writes BOTH `fcmToken` and `fcmTokens` (via `arrayUnion`) — confirmed by reading the current file, not the scan. The REAL, currently-live gap is a TIMING one: `FCMService().initialize()` — the only thing that calls `_saveTokenToFirestore()` — runs exactly ONCE, in `main.dart`, at app cold-start, and only persists a token if `FirebaseAuth.instance.currentUser` is ALREADY non-null at that exact moment. A returning customer who was already signed in before relaunching gets their token saved correctly on relaunch. A customer's FIRST sign-in in a given app session — the common case for a fresh install, or signing in after a logout without restarting the app — does NOT, because `initialize()` already ran (with no user) before the login screen even rendered, and nothing calls it or an equivalent save again afterward anywhere in `apps/marketplace`. `apps/delivery` and `apps/employee` do not have this gap because THEIR OWN `auth_provider.dart` files call a private `_updateFCMToken(uid)` helper explicitly, right after every successful sign-in, independent of `FCMService`'s app-startup timing — confirmed by reading `apps/delivery/lib/providers/auth_provider.dart:62,100,114-131`. `apps/admin` and `apps/seller` have the SAME gap marketplace does (neither has an equivalent per-login save either) but are out of this phase's scope, unchanged from the original contract, because `sendBroadcastNotification`'s intended audience is customers. SEPARATELY, and NOT a schema question at all: `sendBroadcastNotification` (`functions/src/admin/notifications.ts:354`) builds its audience with its OWN inline `doc.data().fcmTokens || []` — reading ONLY the array field, not the file's own shared `uniqueTokens()` helper (`:35-50`) that `sendNotificationToUser`/`sendOrderUpdateNotification` already use, which ALSO checks the singular `fcmToken` as a fallback. This means even a user whose token exists ONLY as the singular field (a stale document from before `fcm_service.dart` started writing both, or a hypothetical future regression) is silently excluded from every broadcast, though currently reached by the two single-recipient functions. **N-17, re-verified unchanged**: `notifications.ts:348` (`admin.firestore().collection("users").get()`) loads the entire collection with no pagination inside a 256MiB callable. In scope: WS1 add an explicit post-login FCM token save to `apps/marketplace/lib/providers/auth_provider.dart`, mirroring `apps/delivery`'s own `_updateFCMToken(uid)` pattern, called from every genuine new-session success path (`registerWithEmail`, `signInWithEmail`, `verifyPhoneOTP`, `signInWithGoogle` — identified by their `_logAuthEvent(..., true, ...)` markers, not by line-number guessing); WS2 make `sendBroadcastNotification` reuse the existing `uniqueTokens()` helper instead of its own array-only inline read; WS3 paginate the user scan. Files expected: `docs/active/BRANCH_DISPOSITIONS.md` (this row), `functions/src/admin/notifications.ts`, `packages/agrimore_services/lib/notifications/fcm_service.dart` (only if WS1 investigation finds a reason to touch it — currently expected NOT to, since it already writes both fields correctly), `apps/marketplace/lib/providers/auth_provider.dart`, `functions/scripts/phase33_broadcast_audience_test.js` (new). `must_not_touch`: `firestore.rules`, `storage.rules`, `functions/src/customer/**`. Any `packages/**` change forces `flutter analyze` in all five apps — not expected to be needed here given the finding above, re-checked once WS1 is implemented. A filename-prefix predicate (`request.auth.uid + '_'`) closes the cross-seller overwrite for the seller branch with ZERO client change, since the existing convention already satisfies it; admin's write path is unaffected. **`delivery_proofs/{fileName}`**: filenames are `{orderId}_{timestamp}.jpg` (`active_order_screen.dart:873`) — no partner-uid prefix, so ownership needs a cross-service Firestore check (`firestore.get(/databases/(default)/documents/orders/$(orderId)).data.deliveryPartnerId`) against the orderId PARSED from the filename via `.split('_')[0]` — closes BOTH N-19's read leak (any authenticated user could read every proof photo) and the write-side overwrite, again with no client change since the existing filename convention already carries what the check needs. **Chat (N-20)**: `chat/{threadId}/{fileName}` gated only on `isAuthenticated()` for both read and write, and accepted any content type. `threads/{threadId}` Firestore documents (written by `apps/delivery` and `apps/seller`) carry a `participantIds` array — scoped both read and write to `participantIds.hasAny([request.auth.uid])` via the same cross-service `firestore.get()` pattern, plus added `isImage()`. **Document paths** (`seller_documents`/`employee_documents`/`delivery_documents`): grepped every app for a write to any of the three — ZERO found. NOT a rules fix: no owner-scoped rule can work for a write that happens before the account exists — the correct fix reorders the signup flow itself (create the account FIRST, then upload to the existing owner-scoped `delivery_documents/{userId}/{fileName}`), which is a client-flow redesign, not a `storage.rules` change, and deserves its own phase rather than being rushed into this one. Registered for a future phase. WS2 has a real, confirmed complication its own `owner_decisions_needed`/`stop_conditions` anticipated: `firebase.json` lists 4 Hosting SITES (`agrimore-66a4e`, `agrimore-66a4e-bb1da`, `agrimore-delivery-partner`, `agrimore-seller-app`), each with a default `*.web.app`/`*.firebaseapp.com` domain pair — BUT `apps/marketplace/lib/app/routes.dart:99` hardcodes `baseUrl = 'https://agrimore.in'`, a CUSTOM domain, used throughout the app for shared product links. Whether `agrimore.in` is the domain Firebase Hosting's custom-domain mapping actually serves marketplace traffic from (vs. a marketing site pointed elsewhere, or a redirect), whether the other three apps have their OWN custom domains not found by this search, and whether a custom domain even reaches this Cloud Function with the SAME `Origin` header a browser sent (vs. something a CDN/redirect layer rewrites) are all things this repository cannot answer with certainty — getting this wrong risks breaking OTP login for real customers on the LIVE production web app, exactly the stop condition the original contract named. WS2 moved to a new `BLOCKED` phase, **FIX-12B**, pending the owner confirming the exact origin list. **WS1 has no such risk and is fully in scope here**: `sendPhoneOTP.ts`/`sendEmailOTP.ts`'s existing daily cap (10/day) is keyed ONLY by phone number / email address — confirmed by re-reading both files fresh — so an attacker iterating through many DIFFERENT numbers/addresses from one source is bound only by the 2Factor/Resend account's own budget, not by anything this codebase enforces. Added a SEPARATE, per-caller (IP-address, SHA-256-hashed before storage — mirrors this codebase's own preference for hashing sensitive identifiers, e.g. OTP hashing itself) daily sliding-window cap in a new `otp_ip_limits/{hashedIp}` collection (Cloud-Functions-only, `allow read, write: if false`, mirroring `phone_otp_codes`/`otp_codes`'s existing pattern exactly), checked alongside the existing per-number/per-address check and incremented only on an actual successful delivery — same semantics as the existing counter. Cap set to 50/day per IP: high enough that a shared IP behind NAT (a real, common scenario — office wifi, a mobile carrier's CGNAT) sees no practical impact under normal use, low enough to bound an enumeration attack's cost to roughly 5x one legitimate user's own daily allowance rather than unbounded. This is a safety-margin engineering choice, not a business-policy one — any reasonable value in a sane range achieves the goal; the owner can retune the one constant. | `agrimore/fix16-createorder-bounds` | `f081ec8` | `MERGED_DEVELOP` | 2026-09-05 | session FIX-16 | Phase FIX-16: bound how fast and how large an authenticated caller can create orders (finding **N-23**). RE-VERIFIED at base `c85258f` (develop tip after the FIX-15 merge), not carried from the contract's `2f5252c` text: read `createOrder.ts` directly — no per-user rate limit exists anywhere in the function, and a COD order costs the caller nothing to create, so an authenticated script can call it without bound; `deliveryAddress`/`notes`/`deliverySlot`/`orderType`/`autoFrequency` are all written into the order document verbatim from client input (`:650-653`) with no shape or size check anywhere before that write. **WS1** (rate limit): FIRST ATTEMPT was a flat per-uid cooldown mirroring `createAssociateOnboardingPayment.ts`'s `onboarding_rate_limits` pattern (`ORDER_RATE_LIMIT_WINDOW_MS = 10_000`, one order per 10s) — REVERTED after the full `gate.sh --full --emulator` battery (68 suites) caught it regressing FOUR existing suites with `failed=4`: `phase14_payment_replay_test`, `phase15_order_integrity_test`, `phase27_stock_integrity_test`, `phaseD_redemption_test` — all of which legitimately call `createOrder` a second time for the SAME uid within seconds (retrying with a different coupon, attempting a double spend that must be rejected for ITS OWN reason, a second real order after stock moved). This is real evidence, not a test-fixture inconvenience: a genuine customer does the same thing (places one order, then immediately places a separate one for something they forgot), so a flat cooldown between ANY two calls was the wrong shape of control. REPLACED with a fixed-window COUNTER: `order_rate_limits/{uid}` now stores `{windowStart, count}`, allowing up to `MAX_ORDERS_PER_WINDOW = 8` successful orders per `ORDER_RATE_LIMIT_WINDOW_MS = 60_000` (60s), still read/written INSIDE `createOrder`'s own existing transaction so the check-then-write stays atomic with the order-creation writes it gates. 8/60s is a generous engineering safety margin, not a measured business ceiling (same class of judgment call as FIX-12's `MAX_SENDS_PER_IP_PER_DAY = 50`) — comfortably above every same-uid call pattern in this codebase's own suites (none exceeds 2), while still turning "unlimited" into a real, enforced ceiling (≤480/hour) against a burst-abuse script. Marked only after every other write for the order has been queued, so a caller who fails validation or hits a precondition later in the same transaction never consumes a window slot for an order that didn't happen. Re-ran all four regressed suites directly after the fix — all pass with their correct rejection reasons, not the rate limiter's. **WS2** (input bounds): `MAX_DELIVERY_ADDRESS_BYTES = 4096` (measured against `AddressModel.toMap()`'s real fields — name/phone/two address lines/city/state/zipcode/country/landmark/lat-lng — which serialize to well under 1 KB), `MAX_NOTES_LENGTH = 500`, `MAX_SHORT_STRING_FIELD_LENGTH = 100` for `deliverySlot`/`orderType`/`autoFrequency`; `deliveryAddress` additionally checked to be a non-array object. Runs before any Firestore read (pure input validation), so an oversized payload fails fast with a clean `invalid-argument` instead of riding through the whole transaction only to hit Firestore's 1 MiB document-size limit at commit time. **WS3**: `functions/scripts/phase36_createorder_bounds_test.js`, 10 scenarios covering both the rate-limit counter (8 back-to-back distinct orders from one uid all succeed, a 9th in the same window is refused and creates no order, a different uid is unaffected, the window resets after it expires) and each input-bounds check (oversized/malformed `deliveryAddress`, oversized `notes`/`deliverySlot`/`autoFrequency`, plus a positive control with real-shape values). `must_not_touch`: `storage.rules`, `apps/**`, `packages/**` (out of scope — WS1/WS2 are entirely server-side). Files expected: `docs/active/BRANCH_DISPOSITIONS.md` (this row), `functions/src/customer/createOrder.ts`, `firestore.rules`, `functions/scripts/phase36_createorder_bounds_test.js`, `.claude/skills/agrimore/scripts/gate.sh` (new suite added to the emulator battery), `.claude/skills/agrimore/references/security.md` (findings register, standard pattern). New: `packages/agrimore_services/lib/ai/ai_connection_service.dart` (thin wrapper: `connect(provider, apiKey)` / `disconnect()` calling AI-1's two callables via `cloud_functions`, `watchStatus(uid)` streaming `ai_connection_status/{uid}`), `apps/marketplace/lib/providers/ai_connection_provider.dart` (ChangeNotifier: `connected`, `provider`, `connectedAt`, `isLoading`, `errorMessage`; `loadStatus()`, `connect()`, `disconnect()`), registered in `main.dart` alongside the other `ChangeNotifierProvider`s (mirrors `WalletProvider`'s registration at `main.dart:170`). UI: provider picker (Gemini/ChatGPT) + masked API-key field + explicit fee disclosure ('AgriMore charges ₹50 once to activate — any usage cost your own Gemini/ChatGPT key incurs is billed to you directly by Google/OpenAI, not AgriMore', matching the owner's own spec wording that the two charges must never be shown mixed together) + a Connect button gated by `DialogHelper.showConfirmation`; when connected, a status row (provider + connected-since) + Disconnect button (confirmation dialog states plainly that disconnecting forfeits the ₹50, no refund, per AI-1's own no-refund default) + a free "change key" affordance reusing the same connect flow. Insufficient-balance path routes the user to the existing wallet top-up flow (`AppRoutes.wallet`), never a raw error. | `agrimore/rfq1-negotiation` | `f633a5d` | `MERGED_DEVELOP` | 2026-09-07 | session RFQ-1 | Phase RFQ-1 (independent of the AI programme; confirmed 2026-09-07 by both the customer-app and seller-app owner specs as a genuine gap — RFQ/negotiation is entirely greenfield, zero matches anywhere in the repo). RE-SCOPED at claim time by reading `createOrder.ts`/`orderPricing.ts` in full (1,379 lines combined): B2B pricing today is ENTIRELY catalog-derived (`product.b2bPrice`/`b2bMoq`/`isB2BEnabled`) with NO existing concept of a per-order custom/negotiated price — `createOrder.ts` is also this codebase's single most security-sensitive, most-collision-prone file (every historical concurrent-session incident touched it). Rather than extend `createOrder.ts` with a new client-triggerable custom-price path in the same phase that also builds the negotiation UI, **this phase is deliberately narrowed to the negotiation mechanism only** — a buyer and seller reach a locked, mutually-accepted price/quantity — and does NOT yet wire an accepted RFQ into `createOrder.ts`. That integration (the actual money-moving step) is split to a new phase RFQ-3, sized and reviewed on its own once this mechanism is proven. Objective: new `rfqs/{rfqId}` collection + three callables (v2 `onCall`, mirroring `createOrder.ts`'s server-derived-everything discipline for whatever it DOES touch): `createRfq({productId, quantity, proposedPrice?, notes?})` (buyer-initiated; validates the product exists and `isB2BEnabled === true` — mirrors `orderPricing.ts:269`'s exact check; resolves `sellerId` from `products/{productId}.sellerId` server-side, never client-supplied; creates the doc `status:'pending'`, `awaitingResponseFrom:'seller'`, appends an append-only `history` entry — mirrors the Product Credit ledger's append-only philosophy over in-place mutation, so a negotiation's full back-and-forth is always reconstructable), `submitRfqOffer({rfqId, price, quantity, notes})` (either party may submit a new offer ONLY when `awaitingResponseFrom` names their own role — derived server-side from whether `request.auth.uid` matches the rfq's `buyerId` or `sellerId`, never a client-asserted role; flips `awaitingResponseFrom` to the other party; appends history), `respondToRfqOffer({rfqId, action:'accept'|'reject'})` (accept locks `finalPrice`/`finalQuantity`/`acceptedAt` from the last offer and sets `status:'accepted'`; reject sets `status:'rejected'`; only the party currently awaited may call this). Rules: `rfqs/{rfqId}` — `allow read: if isOwner(resource.data.buyerId) || isOwner(resource.data.sellerId) || isAdmin()`; `allow write: if false` (CF-only, mirrors `orders/{orderId}`'s `allow create: if false` shape exactly). No client UI this phase (RFQ-2's job) — proven entirely through emulator callable + rules suites, mirroring `phase37`/`phase37b`'s pairing. Files expected: `docs/active/BRANCH_DISPOSITIONS.md` (this row), `functions/src/customer/rfq.ts` (new), `functions/src/index.ts` (export), `firestore.rules` (one new collection block), `firestore.indexes.json` (only if a query needs a composite index — re-verify at build time; a per-buyer/per-seller `rfqs` list view will need one), `functions/scripts/` (two new suites: callable behaviour + rules). `must_not_touch`: `createOrder.ts`/`orderPricing.ts` (RFQ-3's job, not this phase's), `products` rules/schema, any existing order/wallet suite. Lanes: security (a brand-new money-adjacent negotiation surface — no actual balance movement yet, but the LOCKED price this phase produces is exactly what RFQ-3 will later trust, so the locking logic itself must be as rigorously proven as any money path). | `agrimore/fix-v1-trigger-fieldvalue` | `496f360` | `MERGED_DEVELOP` | 2026-09-07 | session P0-FIELDVALUE | **P0 — live money not paid.** Owner-directed (picked up the background task spawned during RFQ-3's security lane, `task_0ca9a0e7`, originally scoped to `orderNotifications.ts`'s crash alone; escalated after investigation revealed a systemic, live, money-impacting bug — the owner explicitly chose "fix everything now" when presented with the finding). ROOT CAUSE, established with direct evidence, not assumed: `admin.firestore.FieldValue`/`.Timestamp` (the legacy namespace-style static property access) is **undefined** specifically inside the isolated child process the Firebase Functions emulator spins up for a REAL, externally-triggered background function invocation (`runBackground`/`processBackground` in firebase-tools' own `functionsEmulatorRuntime.js`) — even though `admin.apps.length === 1` (the app IS initialized) and `require.resolve("firebase-admin")` resolves to the single correct module. Proven via a diagnostic instrumented copy of the COMPILED output (never committed, restored to a byte-identical fresh-`tsc` state immediately after): `Object.keys(admin.firestore)` is `[]` in that context; `require("firebase-admin/firestore").FieldValue` (the MODULAR export) is a real function in the SAME context; switching the crash site to the modular import resolved it completely, re-verified live. This does NOT affect v1/v2 `onCall` callables (client-invoked, a materially different dispatch path — `runFunction` without `runBackground`/`processBackground` in every callable's own stack trace, and this session's own extensive testing of `createOrder.ts`/`rfq.ts`/`payment.ts`/`wallet.ts`/etc. via `test.wrap()` has never once hit this) — confirmed additionally by REPRODUCING THE SAME CRASH via a genuine external Firestore `.update()` write (not `test.wrap()`, which runs in-process and gives FALSE CONFIDENCE — `phase16d1_commission_test.js`'s own "ALL PASSED" result is exactly this false positive) against `payEmployeeCommissionOnDelivery`: a real order transition to `delivered` left `wallet.balance` at `0` and `order.commissionPaid` at `false` — **Sales Associate commissions are not being paid on real, delivered orders in the live production system right now.** SCOPE DECISION (evidence-based, not "fix every file matching the string"): grepped the entire `functions/src` tree for `admin.firestore.(FieldValue|Timestamp|GeoPoint|FieldPath)` — 36 files match, but MOST of them (28) are used ONLY from `onCall` callables (including `createOrder.ts`, `payment.ts`, `wallet.ts`, `rfq.ts` — this codebase's own most security-sensitive, most extensively-proven-working files) and are NOT part of this bug's confirmed dispatch-mechanism class; touching them adds real regression risk to files this codebase has repeatedly, explicitly protected for zero benefit. Fixing ONLY the confirmed-at-risk set: files that themselves define a v1 Firestore trigger or v1/v2 background schedule AND use the pattern, PLUS one shared helper confirmed reached transitively from such a trigger's own call graph (`productCreditLedger.ts`, imported by `productCreditReversal.ts` — itself a `functions.firestore...onWrite` trigger with no DIRECT `FieldValue` reference of its own, so it never appeared in the raw grep, but genuinely at risk through `appendLedgerEntry`'s own use of the pattern; confirmed by reading the import graph, not assumed). Files to fix (9): `admin/notifications.ts` (`onOrderStatusChanged`, CONFIRMED crashed live), `customer/employeeCommission.ts` (`payEmployeeCommissionOnDelivery`, CONFIRMED crashed live, **the P0**), `customer/inventory.ts` (`onProductStockChanged`, CONFIRMED crashed live), `customer/orderNotifications.ts` (`onOrderCreatedNotifications`, CONFIRMED crashed live — the originally-scoped finding), `customer/sellerNotifications.ts` (`notifySellerNewOrder` + `calculateSellerPayout` — the latter is ALSO seller-payout money and was seen entering its real logic body in the same live repro run, at risk though not independently isolated), `customer/productCreditExpiry.ts` (`expireProductCredits`/`releaseExpiredProductCreditHolds`, v1 `functions.pubsub.schedule` — same background-dispatch class by architecture; this environment has no pubsub emulator configured (`firebase.json` declares no pubsub port, confirmed by the emulator's own "function ignored because the pubsub emulator does not exist" messages seen repeatedly this session), so these two cannot be independently reproduced the same way — fixed defensively on the strength of the confirmed pattern-match, disclosed as not independently reproduced), `customer/benefitAccrual.ts` (`accrueMonthlyBenefits`, same pubsub-schedule class, same reproduction limitation), `employee/adminOnboardingActions.ts` (`requestAssociateOnboardingRefundOnSuspend`, a `functionsV1.firestore...onUpdate` trigger — refund-request money-adjacent), `customer/productCreditLedger.ts` (shared helper, transitively at risk per above). Fix: replace every `admin.firestore.FieldValue`/`.Timestamp`/`.GeoPoint`/`.FieldPath` reference in these 9 files with the modular `import { FieldValue, Timestamp, ... } from "firebase-admin/firestore";` equivalent — behaviorally identical when the namespace access DOES work (proven: both produce the exact same sentinel/value), strictly safer when it doesn't. Files expected: `docs/active/BRANCH_DISPOSITIONS.md` (this row), the 9 files above. `may_write`: exactly those 10 paths. `must_not_touch`: the 27 callable-only files sharing the same string pattern (confirmed safe, explicitly left alone — see scope decision above), any RFQ-3 file (separate, unmerged, unrelated worktree). Lanes: security (money correctness, the core reason this phase exists). Collision check 2026-09-07: worktrees = primary `[main]` + `Agrimore-develop` + `Agrimore-rfq3-createorder` (`agrimore/rfq3-createorder-integration`, unmerged, disjoint files — RFQ money-integration work, not this) + this branch; `git branch --list 'agrimore/*fieldvalue*'`/`'*notif*'` = `agrimore/fix10-notification-delivery` (`MERGED_DEVELOP`, unrelated topic) + this branch; `ACTIVE` rows = none before this claim. Deploy consequence: `functions:` all 9 affected functions by explicit name once identified in the build step (a dependency-free, behavior-preserving import-style change to an already-deployed function still requires redeploying that function for the fix to reach production) — **the owner should treat redeploying `payEmployeeCommissionOnDelivery` as urgent** once this phase reaches `E2E_DEVELOP`, since real commission money is not moving in production until that specific function is redeployed. | `agrimore/rfq3-createorder-integration` | `a219ba8` | `MERGED_DEVELOP` | 2026-09-07 | session RFQ-3 | Phase RFQ-3 (final piece of the RFQ backlog; depends on RFQ-1 = `E2E_DEVELOP`). The contract's own note explicitly warned "do not trust this note's line-level assumptions" — re-derived the exact current shape of `createOrder.ts` (926 lines) and `orderPricing.ts` (453 lines) fresh, in full, rather than trusting the placeholder. MAJOR RE-SCOPE from the contract's original plan ("createOrder.ts accepts an optional rfqId..."): `computeOrderPricing` (the function BOTH `createOrder.ts` and `productCreditHold.ts`'s `quoteOrderWithCredit` call, specifically so the two can never disagree on a number — see that file's own header) has NO per-item price-override hook anywhere; every price is either `product.b2bPrice` or `product.salePrice`, selected by `orderMode` alone. Weaving "sometimes this one item uses a THIRD price, from an RFQ" into that shared function risks the exact kind of two-computations-must-agree drift `orderPricing.ts` was extracted specifically to prevent, and touches `createOrder.ts` itself — this codebase's single most security-sensitive, most collision-prone file (every historical concurrent-session incident touched it; SEC/FIX phases 1,3,4,9,16 all hardened it independently). DECIDED: build a NEW, separate callable (`createOrderFromRfq`, new file `functions/src/customer/createOrderFromRfq.ts`) rather than modifying `createOrder.ts`'s own transaction at all — **`createOrder.ts` and `orderPricing.ts` are NOT touched by this phase**, eliminating all regression risk to the general checkout path. The new callable: re-reads `rfqs/{rfqId}` server-side inside its own transaction (fresh, never trusted from the client beyond the id string); validates `status === 'accepted'`, `request.auth.uid === rfq.buyerId` (never a client-asserted role, mirroring `rfq.ts`'s own discipline), and — the double-spend guard — `!rfq.consumedByOrderId`, checked before any write, mirroring `verified_payments.consumedByOrderId`'s exact idempotency-anchor shape; validates the single submitted item's `productId`/`quantity` match `rfq.productId`/`rfq.finalQuantity` EXACTLY; prices the line at `rfq.finalPrice` (never re-derived, never client-supplied) x `rfq.finalQuantity`, with the SAME client-supplied-with-sanity-ceiling `deliveryCharge`/`tax` handling `orderPricing.ts` already uses (an unrelated, already-established "trust but cap" concern, not part of the negotiated price's own integrity); creates exactly ONE order document for `rfq.sellerId` (single product/single seller, no per-seller ratio split needed); forces `orderMode: 'B2B'` (matching `rfq.ts`'s own `isB2BEnabled === true` gate at RFQ-creation time) but explicitly EXEMPTS this path from the normal "B2B requires employeeCode" rule — an RFQ is a direct buyer-seller negotiation with no Sales Associate attribution point anywhere in RFQ-1/RFQ-2, so `employeeUid`/`employeeCode` are always `null` for an RFQ order, a deliberate, disclosed departure from `createOrder.ts`'s own B2B rule, not an oversight; reuses `order_rate_limits/{uid}` (the SAME counter `createOrder.ts` uses — a burst of RFQ-orders should be bounded by the same ceiling, not a separate one), the SAME profile-completeness check, the SAME stock-decrement pattern (one product, `productSnaps`/`productRefs` of length 1), and duplicates (not extracts) `createOrder.ts`'s own payment-verification block (HMAC/live-status already happened in `payment.ts`; this only re-checks the `verified_payments` document) — a deliberate, comment-linked duplication rather than a shared extraction, since unlike pricing, a single self-contained "does this payment cover this payable amount" check has no two-systems-must-agree hazard, and extracting it would require touching `createOrder.ts` to call the extraction, reintroducing exactly the regression surface this design avoids; marks `rfqs/{rfqId}.consumedByOrderId`/`consumedAt` inside the same transaction's write phase. Product Credit holds are explicitly NOT supported on an RFQ order (stacking a hold discount on an already-negotiated bespoke price is an unresolved product question the contract never asked for) — `productCreditHoldId` is simply not accepted by this callable's input shape. Once consumed, an RFQ stays consumed even if the resulting order is later cancelled — mirrors `verified_payments`' own behavior (a consumed payment is never "un-consumed" by a cancellation either; refund/reversal is a separate, manual, out-of-band concern nowhere in this codebase automates for payments, so RFQ consumption is held to the identical standard, not a stricter or looser one) — a deliberate, disclosed simplification, not a silent gap. RULES FINDING (revises the contract's own guess): `rfqs/{rfqId}` already has a BLANKET `allow write: if false` (confirmed by reading `firestore.rules:1393-1396` fresh) — adding `consumedByOrderId` to it needs NO rules change at all, since literally every field is already CF-only. The REAL rules change is elsewhere: `orders/{orderId}`'s three update-guard functions (`ownerCannotChangeOrderFinancials` `:345-349`, `orderFulfilmentCannotChangeProtectedFields` `:428-436`, `deliveryPartnerCanClaimOrder` `:450-457`) are explicit HARDCODED FIELD-NAME denylists, not a blanket-deny-unlisted-fields mechanism (confirmed by reading all three fresh) — the new `rfqId` field this callable writes onto the order document must be added to all three, exactly matching `cto.md`'s own review checklist ("denylists extended for any new financial field in all three rule functions"), or a client update could rewrite an order's RFQ provenance after the fact. CLIENT WIRING DELIBERATELY OUT OF SCOPE, split to a new backlog phase RFQ-4 (mirrors RFQ-1→RFQ-2's own precedent of splitting backend-mechanism from client-UI): read `rfq_detail_screen.dart` fresh — its accepted-RFQ branch already renders `'Accepted — locked at ${finalPrice} x ${finalQuantity}'` as static text with NO purchase action at all; this phase builds and proves the callable exists and is secure, RFQ-4's job is the "Place Order" button that actually calls it. `OrderModel` (marketplace's Dart client) needs NO change: `rfqId` is server-side bookkeeping the model already tolerates being absent from, the same established pattern as `productCreditLedgerEntryId`/`commissionAmount` (confirmed: neither is modeled in `OrderModel` either, per `createOrder.ts`'s own comment). Objective: prove the mechanism end-to-end under the emulator — one-time consumption, price substitution, cross-user/role rejection, item/quantity-mismatch rejection — before any client can reach it. Files expected: `docs/active/BRANCH_DISPOSITIONS.md` (this row), `functions/src/customer/createOrderFromRfq.ts` (new), `functions/src/index.ts` (export), `firestore.rules` (three denylist functions, one field name added to each), `functions/scripts/` (new suite). `may_write`: exactly those four locations. `must_not_touch`: `functions/src/customer/createOrder.ts`, `functions/src/customer/orderPricing.ts` (both confirmed untouched by design, not merely by convention), `functions/src/customer/rfq.ts` (RFQ-1's negotiation logic, read-only from here), any client app (RFQ-4's job), `packages/agrimore_core` (no model change needed). Lanes: security (mandatory per the contract's own framing — this is the highest-stakes phase in the whole RFQ backlog; every invariant here gets the same rigor as I5/I6 in `security.md`). Resolves D-DELIVERY-FEE (owner decision, 2026-09-07, answered via `AskUserQuestion`): per-seller, and each seller picks their own fee shape (flat / slab / distance) for their own orders — the broadest of four options offered, chosen explicitly over a single global shape. VERIFIED_REPOSITORY_FACT (read fresh): `functions/src/customer/orderPricing.ts:358-377` — `deliveryCharge` today is a bare CLIENT-SUPPLIED number for the whole cart with **no server-side source of truth at all**, only a sanity ceiling (`MAX_REASONABLE_DELIVERY_CHARGE = 1000`, line 184); it is then split PROPORTIONALLY across sellers by subtotal share (lines 395-440, the `ratio`/`sellerDeliveryCharge` logic) — the opposite direction from what a per-seller schedule needs (compute each seller's own charge FIRST, then sum for the cart total). `orderPricing.ts` is a **behaviour-preserving extraction** shared verbatim by `createOrder.ts` and `productCreditHold.ts`'s `quoteOrderWithCredit` specifically so the two can never disagree on a number (the file's own header comment) — any change here must keep both callers correct together, exactly the two-computations-must-agree hazard RFQ-3 deliberately avoided by NOT touching this function at all. No `SellerModel` exists in `packages/agrimore_core` (grepped — absent); sellers are raw maps read ad hoc per screen. Checked for an existing seller location field for the "distance" shape: `apps/seller/lib/providers/location_provider.dart:119-120` writes `currentLat`/`currentLng` to the seller doc — but the field name and the provider's own framing ("current") suggest a live/updating GPS position (matching `admin/notifications.ts`'s own `resolvePickupPoint`/`hasFreshPartnerLocation` delivery-partner-style freshness check elsewhere in this codebase), not necessarily a stable "shop address" a customer-facing fee calculation should anchor to — this needs confirmation at build time, not an assumption. **SCOPE DECISION (phased, not a single workstream):** WS1 ships flat and slab shapes only — both need zero location data, are the two simplest of the three, and already give every seller a genuinely real, working, per-seller schedule (satisfying the owner's core ask: "per-seller, seller picks the shape"). WS2 (distance-based) is deferred to its own follow-up sub-phase pending confirmation of what `currentLat`/`currentLng` actually represents and whether it is fit for this purpose, or whether a proper fixed shop-location field needs to be added first — disclosed here, not silently dropped. Resolves D-REFERRAL-TIMING (owner decision, 2026-09-07, answered via `AskUserQuestion`): pay the referrer only after the referred user's first delivered order, not at redemption — the referred user's own signup bonus stays immediate (unaffected; the decision is specifically about referrer payout timing). VERIFIED_REPOSITORY_FACT (read fresh at `functions/src/customer/wallet.ts:398-546`): `redeemReferralCode` currently credits BOTH the referred user's `referredBonus` AND the referrer's `referrerBonus` immediately, inside the same transaction, then writes a `referrals/{id}` doc with `isCompleted: false, completedAt: null` — fields nothing anywhere reads or updates (finding N-44, the reason this phase exists). Investigated the actual delivery pipeline before designing anything: `functions/src/customer/confirmDelivery.ts` (FIX-5's callable) is only ONE of several paths that can set an order to `delivered` — direct client writes from the delivery-partner app and seller/admin panels are STILL PERMITTED (FIX-5B, which would tighten this, is blocked pending owner confirmation of `apps/delivery` adoption) — so the correct hook is a passive Firestore trigger on `orders/{orderId}`, not `confirmDelivery` itself. Found the precedent: `functions/src/customer/employeeCommission.ts:152` (`payEmployeeCommissionOnDelivery`) is exactly this shape — a v1 `.onUpdate` trigger reacting to the transition into a delivered-equivalent status, idempotency re-checked inside its own transaction (`commissionPaid` flag) — the same pattern this phase's own completion flag (`isCompleted`) already exists to support, just never wired to anything. **DESIGN DECISION:** add a new, independent v1 Firestore trigger in `wallet.ts` itself (name TBD at build time, e.g. `completeReferralOnFirstDelivery`) rather than touching `employeeCommission.ts` — Firestore permits multiple independent trigger functions on the same document path, so this needs zero changes to the existing commission trigger. The trigger queries `referrals` for an `isCompleted:false` row keyed by the delivered order's `userId` (cheap no-op for the overwhelming majority of orders, which have no referral at all); "first delivered order" falls out naturally from the `isCompleted` flag's own one-way transition — the FIRST time any of a referred user's orders reaches delivered while their referral is still incomplete IS their first delivered order, by definition, so no separate "is this literally order #1" query is needed. **v1 TRIGGER SAFETY (P0-FIELDVALUE precedent, re-applied deliberately):** `redeemReferralCode` is a v2 `onCall` and safely uses `admin.firestore.FieldValue` — untouched. The NEW trigger is v1, so it must use the modular `import { FieldValue } from "firebase-admin/firestore"` pattern `employeeCommission.ts` already established after the P0 fix, never the crashing namespace-access path; this is a specific, deliberate design constraint, not an incidental detail. **DISCOVERED, DISCLOSED, OUT-OF-SCOPE FINDING:** `employeeCommission.ts`'s own delivered-check reads only `after.orderStatus`, not the dual `orderStatus`-OR-`status` check `confirmDelivery.ts`'s own `statusIsIn()` helper uses specifically because seller-panel/admin writes set only `status` — meaning `payEmployeeCommissionOnDelivery` may already be silently missing commission on every seller/admin-marked delivery, a real, pre-existing gap unrelated to this phase. This phase's OWN new trigger will use the more robust dual-field check (correctness matters for money regardless of an older function's narrower precedent), but does NOT fix `employeeCommission.ts` itself — out of `may_write`, flagged here for a future phase, not silently left undiscovered. **PHASE-NUMBER COLLISION (re-derived, not trusted from the contract):** the contract's own `may_write` names `functions/scripts/phase37_referral_completion_test.js` — stale; `phase37`/`phase37b` are already taken by AI-1's own suites (merged since this contract was planned) and `phase42` was just taken by FIX-8. | `agrimore/fix4b-payout-money-policy` | `6d2d5ea` | `E2E_DEVELOP` | 2026-09-08 | session FIX-4B | Phase FIX-4B — three payout money-policy changes (findings N-9, N-27, N-29), all three explicitly split OUT of FIX-4 pending owner decisions, all three now resolved via `AskUserQuestion` (2026-09-07) and recorded in `decisions.md`: D-COMMISSION-REVERSAL, D-COMMISSION-BASE, D-PAYOUT-MIXED-CATEGORY. Re-read `functions/src/customer/employeeCommission.ts` and `functions/src/customer/sellerNotifications.ts` in full, fresh, at `develop` tip `4014fff` — not from the contract's own stale description (its `base` field, `55c80ad`, predates this session's own P0-FIELDVALUE modular-import fix to both files, confirmed by `git log`). **WS1 — D-COMMISSION-BASE (N-29):** `payEmployeeCommissionOnDelivery`'s `grossAmount` currently reads `after.total` (`orderPricing.ts`'s `grandTotal` = `max(0,subtotal-discount)+deliveryCharge+tax`), so an associate earns commission on delivery charge and tax. The owner decision is "goods subtotal only, excluding delivery charge and tax" — read as `max(0, order.subtotal - order.discount)`, which is arithmetically exactly `order.total - order.deliveryCharge - order.tax` by `orderPricing.ts`'s own per-seller construction (`orderPricing.ts:467-469`) — i.e. the NET goods value actually transacted (discount already netted out, since a coupon-discounted order should not pay full pre-discount commission; delivery/tax excluded per the decision's own words). Forward-looking only, per the decision — no backfill of `commissionAmount` on already-`commissionPaid` orders. **WS2 — D-PAYOUT-MIXED-CATEGORY (N-27):** `calculateSellerPayout` currently reads `data.items[0]?.categoryId` and applies ONE rate (that first item's own category rate) to the seller's ENTIRE order total. Fix: resolve each item's OWN category rate (via the same, already-read-once `categoryRates`/`resolvePayoutRate` — no new Firestore read), sum `commissionAmount = Σ(itemTotal_i × rate_i/100)` across the seller's items, then derive the stored `commissionRate` field as the resulting weighted average (`commissionAmount/grossAmount×100`) for backward-compat with anything reading that field as a single percentage — mathematically identical to, and simpler than, computing a separate weighted-rate-then-multiply step. **WS3 — D-COMMISSION-REVERSAL (N-9):** a NEW trigger, `reverseEmployeeCommissionOnCancellation`, added to `employeeCommission.ts` (co-located with `payEmployeeCommissionOnDelivery`, mirroring how `wallet.ts` already holds both `redeemReferralCode` and `completeReferralOnFirstDelivery`) — fires on the transition into a reversal-equivalent status on an order with `commissionPaid:true` and `commissionReversed` not yet `true`. Read `functions/src/customer/productCreditReversal.ts` in full as the closest existing precedent for "claw back a financial grant on order cancellation" and mirror its shape exactly: a cheap, non-authoritative pre-filter on `after` before opening a transaction; ALL reads first inside the transaction, re-deriving `employeeUid`/`commissionAmount` from the LIVE order document (never trusting `after`) as defence-in-depth against a future write path that forgets a denylist entry; idempotency re-checked AND written inside the same transaction. Deliberately uses the BROADER status set `{"cancelled","refunded","returned","rejected"}` (`confirmDelivery.ts`'s own `NOT_DELIVERABLE` set — the most complete "did not actually complete as a sale" set already established in this codebase) rather than `productCreditReversal.ts`'s narrower cancelled-only check, because D-COMMISSION-REVERSAL's own language ("cancelled/reversed") is broader than the literal string "cancelled" and this is new code with no existing narrower precedent of its own to preserve. Debits `wallets/{employeeUid}.balance` by `commissionAmount` via a plain `FieldValue.increment(-commissionAmount)` — **no floor, no insufficient-balance guard** (unlike `requestEmployeePayout.ts`'s own debit, which DOES guard — deliberately NOT mirrored here, since D-COMMISSION-REVERSAL explicitly says the balance CAN go negative). **v1 TRIGGER SAFETY (P0-FIELDVALUE precedent, applied a third time this session after FIX-8/FIX-15B):** all three files already use, or will use, the modular `import { FieldValue } from "firebase-admin/firestore"` — confirmed `employeeCommission.ts` and `sellerNotifications.ts` both already import it (from the P0-FIELDVALUE phase); the new WS3 trigger reuses that same existing import, adds none of its own. **`wallet_transactions.source` for the reversal:** reused the EXISTING `"commission"` enum value (`packages/agrimore_core/lib/models/wallet_transaction_model.dart`'s `TransactionSource`, confirmed by reading it fresh — a fixed, finite Dart enum, NOT a free-form string) with `type:"debit"` rather than inventing a new `"commission_reversal"` value, which would require a `packages/**` change (forcing a five-app analyze, out of proportion for a label) and would in any case just render as `"adjustment"` client-side on an unrecognized string (`WalletTransactionModel.fromMap`'s own `orElse: () => TransactionSource.adjustment` — confirmed by reading it, not assumed) — `source:"commission"` + `type:"debit"` is MORE informative than that fallback and needs zero `packages/**` touch. | `agrimore/ai4b-seller-ai-proxy` | `863004d` | `E2E_DEVELOP` | 2026-09-08 | session AI-4B | Phase AI-4B — seller-scoped AI proxy callable, mirroring `functions/src/customer/aiChatProxy.ts` (Phase AI-2). Re-read fresh, not from this phase's own claim-time contract text (written at AI-4's merge time, before this investigation): `aiChatProxy.ts` (293 lines, read in full) is a THIN, STATELESS RELAY — it hardcodes a `TOOLS` array (function DECLARATIONS Gemini may choose to call: `searchProducts`/`getOrders`/etc.) and forwards the client's own `contents` array to the Gemini REST API; it never executes a tool call itself. When Gemini's response contains a `functionCall`, this callable just returns `{type:"functionCall", name, args}` back to the CLIENT — the CLIENT (per the file's own header comment, `packages/agrimore_services/lib/ai/ai_chat_service.dart`'s pre-existing `handleXxx` methods, which predate AI-2 entirely — Phase AI-1/AI-2/AI-3 were fixing a hardcoded-Gemini-key hole in an ALREADY-LIVE customer AI chat feature, not building one from scratch) is what actually executes the Firestore query, under the CALLER'S OWN Firebase Auth context, scoped by `firestore.rules`. **This changes what "the seller-scoped authorization boundary" actually means, materially from how the AI-4 claim-time note framed it** ("treat with the same rigor as I15" implied a server-side data-scoping problem to solve here). Checked `firestore.rules` fresh: `products/{productId}` read is `allow read: if true` (public catalog, zero sensitivity — a seller's own products need no scoping at all); `orders/{orderId}` read (line 533) already requires `resource.data.sellerId == request.auth.uid` for the seller branch — an EXISTING, already-deployed, per-document ownership check with ZERO new rules needed. Since Gemini can only ever REQUEST a functionCall (e.g. `getOrderDetails({orderId:"someone-elses-id"})`) and never causes THIS callable to read Firestore for order/product data at all (it only reads the caller's OWN `ai_connections/{uid}` doc, uid-generic, already used identically by AI-1/AI-2/AI-4), a crafted prompt cannot make this specific callable leak another seller's data — the callable is safe by construction, mirroring AI-2's own callable exactly. **REDEFINED SCOPE (disclosed, not silent):** this phase ships ONLY a new `functions/src/seller/aiChatProxy.ts` — a structural clone of AI-2's relay logic (same auth/connection/gemini-only checks, same input-bounds constants, same error handling incl. the "never log the raw axios error, it carries the decrypted key in the request URL" discipline) with a SELLER-APPROPRIATE `TOOLS` array (`getMySellerProducts`, `getMySellerOrderSummary`/`getMySellerOrderDetails`, `getMySellerProfile` — direct analogs of AI-2's own tools, scoped by NAME/DESCRIPTION to steer Gemini toward seller-relevant requests, not by any new server-side enforcement) and a seller-oriented system prompt. The client-side EXECUTION of these tool calls (the actual `sellerId==uid`-filtered Firestore queries, mirroring `ai_chat_service.dart`'s own handleXxx pattern) is EXPLICITLY OUT OF SCOPE here (`must_not_touch: apps/**` unchanged from the original contract) — deferred to whichever phase builds the seller AI chat UI (AI-4C, or a new phase named at that phase's own claim time if AI-4C's own scope doesn't naturally include it). AI-4D (sales analysis/pricing/stock-prediction AGGREGATE tools) remains correctly out of scope: those need genuine server-side computation this thin-relay pattern cannot provide, unlike the basic get-my-X tools here. Files: `docs/active/BRANCH_DISPOSITIONS.md` (this row), `functions/src/seller/aiChatProxy.ts` (new), `functions/src/index.ts` (new export). `must_not_touch` (unchanged from original contract): `apps/**`, `packages/**`, `functions/src/customer/aiConnection.ts` (reused verbatim — `AI_KEY_ENCRYPTION_SECRET`/`decryptApiKey` already exported, uid-generic, confirmed by reading), `functions/src/employee/**`. Lanes: `security` (a new AI-facing surface, even though this specific callable is safe by construction — the lane still applies per `surface.sh` on `functions/**`).
