WS1 (confirm each still referenced by live code) done fresh via grep across all five apps + `functions/src` — all ten have live references, confirmed by count (`subscriptions` 6, `sellerRequests` 7, `scratchCards` 2, `transactions` 2, `recent_searches` 3, `trending_searches` 1, `masterProducts` 2, `centers` 1, `product_price_mappings` 2, `subscription_plans` 2). **Deep field/owner-shape investigation done this tick for 4 of 10 (the rest continues next tick, before any rules are written):** `subscriptions/{id}` — top-level, doc-id auto-generated, `userId` field set to the caller's own uid at BOTH client write sites (`payment_method_screen.dart:309` auto-delivery-at-checkout, `subscription_setup_screen.dart:60` a second, apparently-duplicate creation path — flagging, not fixing, a real schema inconsistency between the two: the checkout path stores `nextRunDate` as a Firestore `Timestamp`, the setup-screen path stores it as an ISO8601 `String` — worth the owner knowing since `subscriptionChecker`, the live source-less function that reads this field, may only handle one shape correctly). Owner's own read (`my_subscriptions_screen.dart:29`) is a `.where('userId', isEqualTo: uid)` query — rules-compatible. Client mutates ONLY `isActive` (both the owner's own toggle in `my_subscriptions_screen.dart:45` AND admin's toggle in `subscription_management_screen.dart:259`) — a value-guarded `hasOnly(['isActive'])` update covers both real call sites exactly, nothing looser. `sellerRequests/{uid}` — doc-id keyed by uid (self-attributed at create, matching `sellers/{uid}`'s own convention), but **currently holds live `bankName`/`accountNumber`/`ifsc` fields at application time** (`seller_apply_screen.dart:79`, both the marketplace and admin copies, and `seller_registration_screen.dart:67`) — the EXACT same PII class FIX-2 (finding N-2) already had to lock down for `sellers/{uid}` itself. This collection needs the SAME rigor: owner-or-admin read only, NEVER public, matching `seller_payout_details/{uid}`'s own established pattern, not `sellers/{uid}`'s public one. Admin's `_approve`/`_reject` flow (`seller_requests_management_screen.dart`) writes only `status`/`reviewedAt` on top of the applicant's own create — a value-guarded admin-or-owner-create, admin-only-status-update shape covers the real usage. `scratchCards` + `transactions` — **NOT top-level collections**, both are `users/{userId}/scratchCards/{cardId}` and `users/{userId}/transactions/{transactionId}` SUB-collections (missed by the phase's own original naming, corrected here) — will nest inside the EXISTING `match /users/{userId}` block alongside `addresses`/`wishlist`/`cart`/etc., not as new top-level blocks. **Self-caught finding, DISCLOSED not fixed in this phase (same class as N-2b/N-6G — a reviewer's own judgement is not an owner decision to fold into an unrelated phase's scope):** `rewards_screen.dart`'s scratch-card claim flow (`_handleClaim()`) reads and unconditionally overwrites `users/{uid}.walletBalance` client-side with NO Cloud Function involved — a completely separate, parallel pseudo-wallet field from the properly-secured `wallets/{uid}.balance` this whole codebase's security model is built around (Invariant I3). Checked `firestore.rules`' own `ownerCannotChangePrivilegedFields()` (the `users/{userId}` update denylist) fresh: `walletBalance` is NOT in it — an authenticated user can ALREADY set their own `users/{uid}.walletBalance` to any value directly, right now, completely independent of whether `scratchCards` gets its own rules block in this phase or not. Exhaustively grepped every other reference to the literal field name `walletBalance` across all five apps to bound the blast radius: `settings_screen.dart` and `mobile_cart_screen.dart` both use a LOCAL variable that happens to share the name but is sourced from `wallet.balance`/`walletProvider.balance` (the real, separate `wallets/{uid}` collection) — NOT this field; the only other touchpoint is `order_service.dart`'s `createWalletTransaction`, confirmed DEAD CODE (grepped for callers across all apps: zero — the same file FIX-15 WS2 already partially gutted for the identical reason, `createOrder`/`validateCoupon`). So the practical blast radius today is confined to a self-contained, display-only "rewards" tally with no confirmed path to real spendable money or a discount at checkout — a real integrity/trust issue (P2, not P0), not a live money-theft vector, but genuinely worth its own small follow-up phase (add `walletBalance` to the denylist, and move scratch-card crediting server-side via a Cloud Function so blocking the client write doesn't just break the feature). NOT fixed here: doing so would exceed this phase's own scope (rules coverage for the ten named collections, not redesigning an unrelated feature's money-crediting architecture) and risks breaking a feature the owner just said to keep, with no replacement mechanism built. **Remaining 6 investigated this tick — WS1 depth now complete for all ten, no rules written yet:** `recent_searches` — sub-collection `users/{uid}/recent_searches/{id}`. **DEAD WIDGET, not just uncovered:** the `RecentSearches` widget class (`recent_searches.dart`) that reads/deletes it is never instantiated anywhere in `apps/marketplace` (grepped the constructor call — zero real usages); the ACTUAL live "recent searches" feature (`mobile_search_screen.dart`) is a completely separate, unrelated SharedPreferences-based local implementation sharing only a similar name. No create call exists anywhere for the Firestore version either — it can only ever be empty in production. `trending_searches` — top-level, same pattern: the `TrendingSearches` widget is ALSO never instantiated anywhere (zero real usages), read-only with a hardcoded fallback list, and nothing anywhere writes to it. Both are being given a rules block anyway per the owner's literal "keep all ten" answer (cheap, safe, future-proofs either widget ever being wired up) rather than unilaterally treating "keep" as not applying to code later discovered to be dead — but flagged clearly: a future phase should decide whether to wire these into the real search screen or delete them, mirroring N-42/N-43's own precedent. `masterProducts` + `centers` — both top-level, seller-read-only reference data (`seller_product_provider.dart`'s `searchMasterProducts`/`loadCenters`, called live from `add_product_screen.dart`); zero writers anywhere in any app or `functions/src` — admin/script-seeded only, matching `product_price_mappings`' own non-seller-suffixed base-price docs (see below). `product_price_mappings/{compositeId}` — top-level, doc id `{masterProductId}_{centerId}_{sellerId}`; the ONLY live writer is `_saveCenterPriceMapping` (called from `add_product_screen.dart` via `addProduct`/`updateProduct`), always self-attributing `sellerId: product.sellerId` from `SellerAuthProvider.currentUser.uid`/`auth.currentUser.uid` — never client-forgeable, confirmed by reading both call sites. `getCenterPrice` also reads a SECOND candidate id shape with no seller suffix (`{masterProductId}_{centerId}`) — a shared, non-seller-specific base price the client never writes (matches the admin/script-seeded pattern above). `subscription_plans` — top-level, admin-only: `apps/admin`'s own subscription-management screen is the ONLY reader or writer anywhere (create + list) — **and, checked fresh, `apps/marketplace`'s actual subscription-creation flows (`subscription_setup_screen.dart`/`payment_method_screen.dart`) never reference `subscription_plans` at all** — a customer's `frequency` is a raw hardcoded string the user picks, not tied to any plan document. This is a half-built feature: an admin can curate a plan catalog that nothing on the customer side currently consumes. Disclosed, not fixed (out of a rules-coverage phase's own scope to wire up a missing customer-side integration). **Final rule design, ready to write next tick:** `subscriptions` — read owner-or-admin (matches the `.where('userId'...)` query + admin's unfiltered read); create: `request.resource.data.userId == request.auth.uid`; update: owner-or-admin, `diff().affectedKeys().hasOnly(['isActive'])`; delete: owner-or-admin. `sellerRequests/{uid}` — read: owner-or-admin ONLY, never public (bank-PII rigor, matching `seller_payout_details/{uid}`, NOT `sellers/{uid}`'s public read); create: owner-self, uid-keyed, `status` must start `'pending'`; update: admin-only, OR owner-only while `resource.data.status == 'pending'` and not touching `status` themselves (mirrors the self-apply-writes-pending-only convention used everywhere else in this file); delete: admin-only. `users/{uid}/scratchCards/{cardId}` (nests inside the existing `match /users/{userId}` block) — read: owner; create: `if false` (nothing client-side ever creates one — presumably seeded by an admin/promo script via the Admin SDK, which bypasses rules regardless); update: owner, `hasOnly(['isScratched'])` only; delete: `if false`. `users/{uid}/transactions/{id}` (same nesting) — read: owner; create: owner (a reward-claim log entry — bounded risk given `walletBalance` itself is a separate, already-disclosed, display-only field); update, delete: `if false`. `recent_searches` (nests under `users/{userId}`) — read, write: owner (mirrors the sibling `addresses`/`wishlist`/`cart` sub-collections' identical shape exactly, despite the dead-widget caveat above). `trending_searches` — read: `if true` (public, non-sensitive aggregate); write: `if false` (explicit deny, matching the `onboarding_rate_limits`-style documented-absence convention, since nothing writes it). `masterProducts`, `centers` — read: `if isAuthenticated()`; write: `if false`. `product_price_mappings/{id}` — read: `if isAuthenticated()`; create, update: `if isAuthenticated() && request.resource.data.sellerId == request.auth.uid` (the rule itself naturally blocks a client from ever writing the non-seller-suffixed base-price docs, since those never carry a matching `sellerId`); delete: `if false`. `subscription_plans` — read, write: `isAdmin()` only. **WS3+WS5 done (2026-09-08, commit `a02023d`):** all ten rules blocks written exactly per the design above; `functions/scripts/phase31_collection_coverage_test.js` — 44 scenarios, all PASSED fresh. Revert-and-watch on the highest-stakes rule (`sellerRequests`' bank-PII read protection): widened it to a bare `isAuthenticated()`, reran — exactly the one PII-cross-read scenario flipped, all 43 others stayed green; restored, reran clean. `gate.sh` fresh: failed=0. **SECURITY LANE (2026-09-08) — PASS_WITH_FINDINGS, one real open risk, two minor/non-findings, none BLOCKING:** (1) **[OPEN, owner verification needed]** before this phase, `subscriptions/{id}` had ZERO rules coverage — `allow create: if false` by default-deny — so no client could EVER create a subscription document; this phase's own `create` rule is the FIRST time this path has ever worked. The created doc carries fully client-supplied `price`/`productId`/`quantity` (only `userId` is validated), and is read by the LIVE, deployed `subscriptionChecker` function — one of the 6 orphan functions with NO source anywhere in this repository (confirmed by grep across `functions/src`: zero matches). Whether that function re-derives price server-side from `products/{productId}.salePrice` (safe) or trusts the client-supplied `price` field (a real underpricing vector on every future recurring order) CANNOT be verified from this repo — `firebase functions:list` was attempted read-only and failed (network/auth in this environment), but the finding rests on the SOURCE not existing, independent of that. Not fixed here (inventing speculative server-side validation against unknown logic risks guessing wrong either direction); recommended the owner view `subscriptionChecker`'s actual deployed source via the Firebase Console before/shortly after this rules change ships, and open a dedicated follow-up phase if it turns out to trust client pricing. (2) **[Minor, disclosed]** `users/{uid}/transactions` create has no value bound on `amount`/`type` at all — traced every reader (only `rewards_screen.dart`, display-only, never reads its own history back for any decision) — confined to a user fabricating a cosmetic-only entry in their own private history, no other party relies on it; not fixed (out of scope, the real writer never needed a tighter bound). (3) **[Checked, non-exploitable]** `product_price_mappings`' `productId` field isn't cross-validated against the doc id's own `sellerId` segment — traced every read (`getCenterPrice`, always scoped by the CALLING seller's own uid) and confirmed a seller can only ever read back price data filed under their own uid segment regardless of the `productId` value inside — no cross-seller impact possible. Grepped `apps/marketplace`, `apps/seller`, `functions/src` for "follow" as a feature: zero matches — genuinely greenfield, no partial prior implementation to reconcile against. `products` are created via a direct client-side Firestore `.add()` (`apps/seller/lib/providers/seller_product_provider.dart:68`), not a callable — so the new-product-follow notification needs a Firestore v1 `products/{productId}.onCreate` trigger, matching this codebase's own established trigger convention already used by `functions/src/customer/sellerNotifications.ts` and `orderNotifications.ts` (`functions.firestore.document(...).onCreate`). **Correction to the earlier decisions.md phrasing**: `sendBroadcastNotification`/`sendNotificationToUser` (`functions/src/admin/notifications.ts:351,454`) are BOTH `requireAdmin(context)`-gated `onCall` callables — a background Firestore trigger has no admin `context.auth` and cannot call either directly, so "reusing" them literally is not possible; confirmed the actually-reusable piece is `createNotificationMessage` (`functions/src/common/helpers.ts`, already a shared exported helper) plus this codebase's own established pattern of a small per-file duplicated `uniqueTokens()` helper (already duplicated independently in `admin/notifications.ts` and `customer/orderNotifications.ts` — matching that existing convention rather than inventing a new shared util). In-app notifications will write to the already-correctly-scoped `users/{uid}/notifications` sub-collection (confirmed `isOwner()`-only per `security.md`'s own N-6 finding from an earlier phase). **Scope for this phase**: (1) a new customer-facing business profile screen (seller name/photo/description + their product grid); (2) a "Sold by `<seller>`" discovery link on `product_details_screen.dart` (without an entry point the feature is unreachable — matching this session's own repeated dead-widget findings on `recent_searches`/`trending_searches`, a lesson deliberately applied here rather than repeated); (3) a new top-level `follows/{followerId}_{sellerId}` collection (composite doc id, matching this codebase's own established composite-key convention from `product_price_mappings`) with follow/unfollow UI; (4) `firestore.rules` coverage for `follows` (self-attributed create/delete only, no forged follows, no self-follow, read: follower/followed-seller/admin, update: `if false`); (5) the new `products/{productId}.onCreate` trigger notifying every follower on BOTH channels (in-app doc + FCM push) per D-BUSINESS-PROFILE-SHAPE. `sellers/{uid}` = `{name, mobile, email, shopName, shopAddress, status}` only — confirmed again, no photo/description field exists. `apps/seller/lib/screens/home/add_product_screen.dart` already has a complete, reusable image-upload flow (`image_picker` gallery pick at quality 82/maxWidth 1600 → bytes → `FirebaseStorage.instance.ref().child('product_images/{sellerId}_{timestamp}.{ext}')` → `putData` with contentType → `getDownloadURL`) — this phase mirrors it exactly for post images (`business_posts/{sellerId}_{timestamp}.{ext}`) rather than inventing a new upload flow. `storage.rules`' existing `product_images/{fileName}` block (`allow read: if true; allow write: if isValidFileSize() && isImage() && (isAdmin() || (isSeller() && fileName.matches(request.auth.uid + '_.*')))`) is the exact pattern mirrored for the new `business_posts/{fileName}` path — the uid-prefixed filename convention naturally matches this phase's own upload path. `apps/seller`'s own `SellerShell` (`screens/shell/seller_shell.dart`) is confirmed a FIXED 5-tab bottom nav (Dashboard/Products/Orders/Earnings/Profile) with no existing 6th slot — post authoring will hang off an existing screen (a button/FAB on `SellerProductsScreen` or `DashboardScreen`, decided at BUILT time) rather than add a new persistent tab, a deliberately smaller navigation footprint than a full new section would need. **Scope for this phase**: (1) a new top-level `business_posts/{postId}` collection — `sellerId` self-attributed, optional `text`/`imageUrl`/`productId` (at least one required so an empty post can't be created), `createdAt`; (2) `firestore.rules` for `business_posts` — public read (matches the existing public-catalog pattern for `products`/`sellers`; personalization to "sellers you follow" is a client-side query filter, not a server access restriction), self-attributed create requiring an approved seller and at least one content field, delete by the owning seller or admin, no update (posts are immutable in this slice); (3) `storage.rules` for `business_posts/{fileName}`, mirroring `product_images` exactly; (4) a seller-side post composer (text + optional image + optional "tag one of my products" picker reusing the seller's own already-loaded product list); (5) a customer-side feed screen (alongside the existing `business_profile_screen.dart`) showing posts from followed sellers only, newest first, each post showing the seller's `shopName` + content + a tappable product tag (deep-links to `product_details_screen.dart`) or a link to the seller's own business profile. **Feed query approach, decided at claim time**: a customer's followed-seller IDs come from `follows.where('followerId','==',uid)` (already proven working); the feed itself queries `business_posts.where('sellerId','in', <that list>).orderBy('createdAt','desc')` — Firestore's `in` operator caps at 30 values, so a customer following more than 30 sellers would only see posts from the first 30 in this v1; disclosed as an honest, acceptable v1 limit (no customer follows anywhere near 30 sellers today, since the whole `follows` collection is brand-new), deliberately NOT solved with a denormalized fan-out collection that has nothing real to justify its own complexity yet. | `agrimore/fix-n6f-referral-code-collision` | `0043dc7` | `E2E_DEVELOP` | 2026-09-11 | session FIX-N6F | Phase FIX-N6F — close security.md finding N-6F: `wallets/{userId}` CREATE leaves `referralCode` unconstrained — `walletBalanceFieldsAreZero()` never covered it — so a self-registering user can set it to ANY string, including a code someone else already publicises (referral-hijacking, since `redeemReferralCode`'s own `.limit(1)` query then has no way to know which of two colliding documents is the real one). **Fresh investigation went further than the finding's own text**: read the ACTUAL generation algorithm (`packages/agrimore_core/lib/models/wallet_model.dart`'s `_generateReferralCode`) — `<4-char name prefix><2-digit userId.hashCode % 100>` — and confirmed this is not just tamperable but genuinely COLLISION-PRONE even for two HONEST users: only 100 possible sequence values per name prefix, and `String.hashCode` is not guaranteed stable across Dart/platform versions. This is a real product-correctness gap, not only a malicious-client one. **Fix**: move code generation entirely server-side. New v1 Firestore trigger `assignReferralCode` (`wallets/{userId}.onCreate`) reads the caller's own `users/{userId}.name` for the same personalized prefix (falls back to a generic prefix on any read failure — a profile-read hiccup must never block code assignment), then generates a candidate (`<prefix><4 random unambiguous alphanumeric chars>`, ~1.6M combinations per prefix — no `0/O/1/I` to avoid transcription confusion when a seller reads a code aloud) and checks uniqueness via `wallets.where('referralCode','==',candidate).limit(1)` before committing, retrying up to 8 times, with a fully-random 8-char fallback if every attempt somehow collides. `firestore.rules`: `referralCode` added to `walletBalanceFieldsAreZero()` (must be absent or `''` at create — the client can no longer set ANY value, closing the squatting vector completely, not just narrowing it). Client: `WalletModel.empty()` no longer calls the old generator (deleted — its only caller); the wallet doc is created with `referralCode: ''`, and the ALREADY-EXISTING `wallets/{uid}.snapshots()` listener (`wallet_provider.dart`, unchanged) picks up the server-assigned code automatically once the trigger writes it — no new client polling logic needed. `may_write`: `functions/src/customer/wallet.ts` (new trigger, appended to the existing file that already owns `redeemReferralCode`/`creditSignupBonus`), `functions/src/index.ts` (export only), `firestore.rules` (one line in `walletBalanceFieldsAreZero()`), `packages/agrimore_core/lib/models/wallet_model.dart` (`empty()` + delete `_generateReferralCode`), `apps/marketplace/lib/providers/wallet_provider.dart` (stop passing the now-unused `userName` argument), `functions/scripts/phase50_referral_code_collision_test.js` (new). `must_not_touch`: `redeemReferralCode`/`completeReferralOnFirstDelivery` (unchanged, already correct once codes are actually unique), every other model/provider, every other app. **`packages/agrimore_core` is a shared package — every app depends on it, so this phase requires the five-app `flutter analyze`, not `apps/marketplace` alone**, per the skill's own standing rule for any `packages/**` change. Lanes: `security` (declared upfront). | `agrimore/fix-l1l2-surface-hardening` | `6f3f2a1` | `E2E_DEVELOP` | 2026-09-11 | session FIX-L1L2 | Phase FIX-L1L2 — close security.md tooling findings **L-1** and **L-2**, both raised against `.claude/skills/agrimore/scripts/surface.sh` and left open since FIX-2/FIX-1 (2026-09-05), re-confirmed still open by reading the current script fresh before claiming. **L-1**: when the positional `git diff --name-only "$BASE".."$HEAD"` (and its space-form fallback) both fail — e.g. a malformed invocation such as a positional path list instead of `<base> <head>` — the script currently swallows the failure: `FILES` ends up empty either way, so a genuine "diff computation failed" is printed as `LANES: none`, byte-identical to a real "no lanes, no changed files" result. A caller cannot tell the two apart, which means a security-relevant change run through a broken invocation could silently skip its lane with no error signal. **Fix**: capture the exit status of the diff attempt(s) explicitly; when both fail (and `--files` was not used), print a clear error to stderr naming the base/head that failed and exit non-zero, instead of falling through to the current unconditional `LANES: none`. A genuinely empty, successful diff still prints `LANES: none` and exits 0 — only the failure path changes. **L-2**: the script's own `security` surface case list (case statement covering checkout/cart/wallet/auth/onboarding/employee screens) does not include `apps/*/lib/screens/admin/sellers/*` or `apps/*/lib/screens/profile/*` — confirmed live during FIX-2 (2026-09-05) when the security lane on that phase ran only because `firestore.rules` happened to be in the same commit; a phase touching only `apps/admin/lib/screens/admin/sellers/seller_requests_management_screen.dart` or `apps/seller/lib/screens/profile/seller_profile_screen.dart` alone — the exact two files FIX-2 itself found copying/rendering seller bank account numbers and IFSC codes — would silently skip the security lane entirely. **Fix**: add both globs to the same `security` case arm. `may_write`: `.claude/skills/agrimore/scripts/surface.sh` only. `must_not_touch`: every other script (`gate.sh`, `state.sh`, `merge-develop.sh`, `promote.sh`), every app/package/function file (a pure tooling-script fix, nothing it lints or classifies is itself touched). Lanes: `skill` only (`surface.sh` on itself would report `skill`, confirmed at BUILT time against the true base) — this is a shell-script change to the review tooling itself, not app/rules/functions/security-surface code; no security/uiux/feedback review applies to the tool's own source, matching the precedent set by FIX-GATE-1 (`gate.sh` dispatch-table fix, also `Lanes: none`/tooling-only). Collision check 2026-09-11: `git worktree list` = primary `[main]` + `Agrimore-develop` only before this claim; `git branch --list 'agrimore/*surface*' 'agrimore/*l1*' 'agrimore/*l2*'` = none; no `ACTIVE`/`CLAIMED` row for this scope. Base: `develop` at `479b3c8`. Deploy consequence: NONE — a shell script used only by this skill's own local review tooling, never deployed to Firebase, never shipped in any app or functions bundle. Identity verified: `Agrimore <agrimorein@gmail.com>` (author + committer). `npm ci` (functions) confirmed clean at claim time (standing precondition; this phase touches neither `functions/` nor any Dart package, `flutter pub get` not required for a pure-shell change). `gate.sh --baseline`: `failed=0` (`functions:build` 0, five-app `analyze` errors=0 across the board — ambient baseline, unaffected by this phase's scope). **BUILT (2026-09-11):** WS1 `6f3f2a1` — both fixes in one commit to the one `may_write` file: `git diff --name-only "$BASE".."$HEAD"` and its space-form fallback now each capture their own exit status; when both fail (and `--files` was not used) the script prints the failing base/head plus git's own error to stderr and exits 1, instead of falling through to the previous unconditional `LANES: none`/exit 0; `apps/*/lib/screens/admin/sellers/*` and `apps/*/lib/screens/profile/*` added to the `security` case arm. `bash -n` syntax check clean. Revert-and-watch on L-1 (the load-bearing check): stashed the fix, re-ran `surface.sh nonexistent-ref-xyz also-fake-ref` — the OLD script prints git's own `fatal: ambiguous argument...` to stderr but still prints `LANES: none` and exits 0, confirming the exact silent fail-open the finding describes; unstashed (restored byte-identical, confirmed via `git status --porcelain`), re-ran the same invocation — NEW script prints the same git error plus its own explicit message and exits 1. Functional checks: a real `develop..HEAD` diff still classifies normally and exits 0; `develop develop` (a genuinely empty, successful diff) still prints `LANES: none` and exits 0 — only the failure path changed, not the legitimate empty-result path. `--files` with `apps/admin/lib/screens/admin/sellers/seller_requests_management_screen.dart` and `apps/seller/lib/screens/profile/seller_profile_screen.dart` (the exact two files FIX-2 named) now classify `security,uiux,feedback` instead of `uiux,feedback` — L-2 confirmed closed. `gate.sh` after WS1: `failed=0`. **LANES (2026-09-11):** `surface.sh` against the true base (`479b3c8`) reported `skill, docs` only — matches the claim's own declared reasoning exactly: a shell-script change to the review tooling itself is not app/rules/functions/security-surface code, so no security/uiux/feedback review applies to the tool's own source (same precedent as FIX-GATE-1). No lane review required or run. **VERIFY (2026-09-11): GO.** `git diff --name-status 479b3c8..HEAD` = exactly the 2 declared files (`surface.sh` + this ledger) — `must_not_touch` honoured (`gate.sh`/`state.sh`/`merge-develop.sh`/`promote.sh` untouched; zero app/package/function files touched). | `agrimore/fix-n18a-delivery-kyc-upload-order` | `3a51ba2` | `E2E_DEVELOP` | 2026-09-12 | session FIX-N18A | Phase FIX-N18A — close security.md finding **N-18a**: delivery partner registration's KYC uploads have been silently failing for as long as the flow has existed. Re-read `apps/delivery/lib/screens/auth/partner_registration_screen.dart` fresh before claiming and confirmed still true: `_submitRegistration()` calls `_uploadImage()` for aadhaar front/back, selfie and license images FIRST, targeting `delivery_partners/{category}/{fileName}` — a path with ZERO `storage.rules` coverage (falls through to the catch-all `match /{allPaths=**} { allow read, write: if false; }`) — and does so BEFORE `FirebaseAuth.instance.createUserWithEmailAndPassword(...)` runs at all, so `request.auth` is `null` at upload time regardless. Each `_uploadImage()` call catches its own error and returns `null` silently (`catch (e) { debugPrint(...); return null; }`); the four resulting URLs (all `null`) are written straight into `DeliveryPartnerModel.aadhaarFrontImage` etc. with no check, and the registration proceeds to a "Registration Submitted" success dialog regardless — meaning every delivery partner who has ever registered has a `delivery_partners/{uid}` document with KYC image fields silently `null`, and the admin approval screen has never had a real document to review, yet nothing has ever surfaced this to the partner OR the admin. The correctly-scoped destination already exists and is unused: `storage.rules:195` `match /delivery_documents/{userId}/{fileName} { allow read: if isAuthenticated() && (request.auth.uid == userId || isAdmin()); allow write: if isAuthenticated() && request.auth.uid == userId && isValidFileSize() && isImage(); }` — added by FIX-11 specifically anticipating this fix, confirmed zero current writers via grep. **Fix**: reorder the flow — create the Firebase Auth account FIRST, then upload all four images to the now-real, owner-scoped `delivery_documents/{uid}/{fileName}` (flat single-segment filenames, e.g. `aadhaar_front_<ts>_<name>`, since the rule's `{fileName}` variable matches exactly one path segment — no subfolder). Explicitly check all four upload results are non-null before writing Firestore documents; if any upload fails OR any step after account creation throws, delete the just-created Firebase Auth user (a fresh session, so `delete()` needs no reauthentication) so a failed registration never leaves an orphaned account blocking that email/phone from a clean retry — closing the exact partial-registration risk the finding's own text flagged as the reason this wasn't rushed into FIX-11. `may_write`: `apps/delivery/lib/screens/auth/partner_registration_screen.dart` only (`_uploadImage`'s signature, `_submitRegistration`'s ordering + rollback). `must_not_touch`: `storage.rules` (the destination rule already exists and is correct, confirmed fresh — untouched), `apps/admin/lib/screens/admin/delivery/*` (reads these fields only as opaque URL strings via `DeliveryPartnerModel`, confirmed via grep — no path-format assumption anywhere to break), `packages/agrimore_core/lib/models/delivery_partner_model.dart`, every other app/screen. Lanes: `security` (declared upfront — an auth-account-creation-ordering + storage-path fix touching KYC document handling). Collision check 2026-09-12: `git worktree list` = primary `[main]` + `Agrimore-develop` only before this claim; `git branch --list 'agrimore/*n18*' 'agrimore/*delivery*kyc*' 'agrimore/*partner-reg*'` = none; no `ACTIVE`/`CLAIMED` row for this scope. Base: `develop` at `e2cbfe7`. Deploy consequence: NONE for functions/rules (client-only change, `storage.rules` already covers the destination path) — a Play release for `apps/delivery` is required for the fix to reach any real user, since this is entirely a client-flow correctness fix with no server-side component to gate it. Identity verified: `Agrimore <agrimorein@gmail.com>` (author + committer). `npm ci` (functions) confirmed clean at claim time (standing precondition; this phase touches only Dart, no `functions/` file). `gate.sh --baseline`: `failed=0` (`analyze:delivery` errors=0 warnings=3 infos=30 — ambient baseline). **HONEST LIMIT, disclosed before building**: `apps/delivery` has no Flutter widget-test harness (`apps/marketplace/test` is this repo's only Dart suite, per `CLAUDE.md`), and this phase cannot exercise the real signup flow live — that would create a genuine Firebase Auth account, Storage object and Firestore document in `agrimore-66a4e`, forbidden by Absolute Rule 4 regardless of who or what triggers it. Verification here rests on `flutter analyze` (compile correctness) and disclosed, careful source-level reasoning about the ordering and rollback, not an executed test — this will be stated plainly at VERIFY, not glossed over. **BUILT (2026-09-12):** WS1 `3a51ba2` — `_uploadImage(uid, file, label)` now targets `delivery_documents/$uid/${label}_<ts>_<name>` (flat, single extra segment, matching `{fileName}`'s one-segment constraint); `_submitRegistration()` reordered to create the auth account first, upload second, with an explicit `if (aadhaarFrontUrl == null || ...) throw Exception(...)` gate before any Firestore write; the `catch` block now deletes `credential.user` (best-effort, wrapped in its own `try/catch`) and signs out before showing the error snackbar. `flutter analyze` on `apps/delivery`: `errors=0 warnings=3 infos=30` — byte-identical to the ambient baseline, and the two `info`-level lints on this file (`unnecessary_import`, `deprecated_member_use` on an unrelated `DropdownButtonFormField`) are pre-existing, confirmed by their line numbers falling outside this diff's own hunks. `gate.sh` after WS1: `failed=0`. **LANES (2026-09-12):** `surface.sh` against the true base (`e2cbfe7`) reported `security, uiux, feedback, docs` — the claim only declared `security`; `uiux`/`feedback` fired because the file is under `apps/*/lib/screens/auth/*`, run anyway per the standing rule (same under-declaration shape as FIX-N50). Full detail `~/.agrimore/run/agrimore/logs/fixn18a_security_uiux_feedback_lanes.md`. **SECURITY = PASS**: the reordered flow satisfies `delivery_documents/{userId}/{fileName}`'s own `request.auth.uid == userId` requirement by construction (upload only ever happens after the matching account is the live session); the new destination is strictly more restrictive than the old always-denied one, not a widening; `credential.user.delete()` can only ever delete the CURRENTLY signed-in user, structurally incapable of touching any other account; the four-way null gate closes the exact silent-failure this finding described. Disclosed, not hidden: if the rollback's own `delete()` call itself fails (a second, separate network failure exactly there), the new account can be left orphaned with no partner profile — strictly no worse than today's universal-failure status quo, and not engineered further as disproportionate to this phase's scope. **UIUX = PASS**: zero new color literals in the diff. **FEEDBACK = PASS**: the one new user-facing string is a plain, safe, purpose-written sentence; zero new `.toString()`/`e.message` leak patterns in the diff — if anything an improvement (a real failure now surfaces, where previously it was silently swallowed into a false "Registration Submitted"). | `agrimore/onboard1-web-payment-handoff` | `2fef645` | `E2E_DEVELOP` | 2026-09-12 | session ONBOARD-1 | Phase ONBOARD-1 — owner-requested feature, not a security-register item: give the marketplace MOBILE app a real "Pay on Web" button for the associate onboarding fee, instead of the current static message telling the visitor to go to `agrimore.in` manually. `OnboardingPaymentStep`'s payment button itself is deliberately `kIsWeb`-gated (Phase 16B-2, D2 — Razorpay checkout must never run inside a mobile build for Play-safety) — mobile currently renders only a `Container` with the text "Please complete the one-time onboarding fee on the AgriMore website (agrimore.in) — the app does not process this payment", no link, no button; the visitor has to type the domain in manually and sign in AGAIN with phone OTP on the website even though they are already signed in on the phone. **Owner's own explicit design for the fix**: an in-app button that opens the phone's SYSTEM DEFAULT BROWSER (`LaunchMode.externalApplication`, never an in-app WebView) at the same onboarding page, already authenticated, carrying the session across. **Chosen mechanism (recommended over the owner's two other named alternatives — a separate app, or a new `associate.agrimore.in` subdomain — both rejected in chat before this claim: a second app would duplicate the entire, already-verified `createAssociateOnboardingPayment` → Razorpay → `verifyRazorpayPayment` → `activateAssociateOnboarding` chain in a second codebase; a new subdomain needs new DNS + a new Firebase Hosting site for zero functional gain, since the existing `agrimore-66a4e` hosting site already serves this exact, unauthenticated-reachable route today)**: a short-lived, single-use, opaque handoff CODE — never a raw Firebase custom token or ID token placed in a URL (a bearer credential in a URL ends up in browser history and server logs; an opaque code that is worthless after one redemption is the safe version of the same idea, the same pattern Firebase's own email-link sign-in relies on). Flow: mobile calls a new `createOnboardingWebHandoff` callable (mints `crypto.randomBytes(32).toString('hex')`, stores `onboarding_handoffs/{code}` = `{uid, purpose:'associate_onboarding', used:false, createdAt, expiresAt: now+5min}`) → opens `${AppRoutes.baseUrl}${AppRoutes.associateOnboarding}?handoff=<code>` (`https://agrimore.in/employee/onboarding?handoff=<code>`) in the external browser via `url_launcher` (already a dependency; the SAME `launchUrl(uri, mode: LaunchMode.externalApplication)` idiom already used for payment/maps links in `checkout_screen.dart`/`payment_method_screen.dart`) → the web build's `AssociateOnboardingScreen`, on load, reads a new `handoffCode` (parsed from the route's query string by `routes.dart`, mirroring the file's own existing `/search?q=` pattern — `AssociateOnboardingScreen` is currently the ONLY branch of the routing switch that cannot carry a query string at all, since Dart's `switch` requires exact string equality against the bare `'/employee/onboarding'` constant) and calls a new `redeemOnboardingWebHandoff` callable, which — inside a single Firestore transaction (look-up + mark-`used`-atomically, so two near-simultaneous redemption attempts, e.g. a page reload firing this twice, cannot both succeed from the same still-valid code) — verifies not-yet-used and not-expired, mints `admin.auth().createCustomToken(uid)`, and returns it; the web page calls `FirebaseAuth.instance.signInWithCustomToken(...)`, and the ALREADY-REACTIVE `_EmployeeStateGate`/`AuthProvider` machinery (unchanged) resumes exactly at the payment step with no new UI to build there. A failed/expired/reused code fails SILENT-and-SAFE: the visitor simply lands on the existing signed-out "Sign In" prompt already on this page, never a dead end. `may_write`: `functions/src/employee/onboardingWebHandoff.ts` (new, both callables), `functions/src/index.ts` (2 exports), `firestore.rules` (new `onboarding_handoffs/{code}` block, `allow read, write: if false` — Admin-SDK-only, mirrors `verified_payments`'s own idiom exactly, no client of any role ever needs to touch this collection directly), `apps/marketplace/lib/screens/employee/onboarding/widgets/onboarding_payment_step.dart` (replace the static mobile `Container` with a real button + `_startWebHandoff()`), `apps/marketplace/lib/screens/employee/onboarding/associate_onboarding_screen.dart` (`handoffCode` constructor param + `_tryRedeemHandoff()` in `initState`), `apps/marketplace/lib/app/routes.dart` (query-string parsing for this one route, mirroring the existing `/search?` special case). `must_not_touch`: `createAssociateOnboardingPayment.ts`/`activateAssociateOnboarding.ts`/`activationCore.ts`/`razorpayOnboardingWebhook.ts`/`reconcileStaleOnboardingPayments.ts` (the payment chain itself is untouched — this phase only bridges the visitor TO it), `onboarding_payment_step.dart`'s WEB branch (`_startPayment`/`_handleModalSuccess`, unchanged), every other route, every other app. Lanes: `security` (a new sign-in-bypass-adjacent mechanism — a fresh trust boundary deserves its own review regardless of how narrow), `functions` (`functions/src/employee/onboardingWebHandoff.ts`), `rules` (`onboarding_handoffs`). Not `uiux`/`feedback` per `surface.sh`'s current case list — to be confirmed against the true base at BUILT time regardless, per the standing rule that a phase never runs less than `surface.sh` detects. Collision check 2026-09-12: `git worktree list` = primary `[main]` + `Agrimore-develop` only before this claim; `git branch --list 'agrimore/*onboard*' 'agrimore/*handoff*'` = none; no `ACTIVE`/`CLAIMED` row for this scope. Mechanism: a new unauthenticated callable-equivalent, `resolveGoogleIdentity` (v1 `onRequest`, mirroring `verifyPhoneOTP.ts`'s own shape exactly, since this function is by necessity called with NO Firebase Auth context yet in either the linked or unlinked case), verifies the Google ID token server-side via `google-auth-library`'s `OAuth2Client.verifyIdToken` (added as an explicit direct dependency at its already-resolved transitive version `9.15.1` — confirmed present in `functions/package-lock.json` before adding, not guessed), extracts the Google subject, and looks up an existing linked Firebase user via `admin.auth().getUserByProviderUid('google.com', subject)` — confirmed present in the installed `firebase-admin@13.8.0` type definitions (`base-auth.d.ts:182`) before writing any code, not assumed. Returns only `{linked: boolean, expectedUid?: string}`. Client: for a linked identity, `signInWithCredential` resolves directly to the existing UID (no new code needed — Firebase Auth's own provider resolution already does this correctly); for an unlinked identity, the Google credential is held in memory only (never persisted) while the AUTH-2 sheet shows a new `GOOGLE_REQUIRES_PHONE_VERIFICATION` state reusing the existing phone/OTP UI verbatim, and `linkWithCredential` attaches Google to the resulting phone-verified UID only after `verifyPhoneOTP` succeeds. `sendPhoneOTP.ts`/`verifyPhoneOTP.ts` are explicitly `must_not_touch` — reused byte-for-byte. Expected to touch: `functions/src/customer/resolveGoogleIdentity.ts` (new), `functions/src/index.ts` (export), `functions/package.json` (one explicit dependency line), `packages/agrimore_services/lib/auth/auth_service.dart` (additive — new marketplace-specific methods; the existing `signInWithGoogle()` used elsewhere is left untouched unless grep proves zero remaining callers), `apps/marketplace/lib/providers/auth_provider.dart`, `apps/marketplace/lib/screens/auth/login_screen.dart`. Zero lines changed in the existing `signInWithGoogle()` (217 insertions, 0 deletions — confirmed by the commit stat, not asserted). One real mistake caught by analyze before commit: `GoogleAuthProvider.credentialFromResult(result)` does not exist in the installed `firebase_auth@5.7.0` (`undefined_method`) — fixed by reading the actual platform-interface source (`firebase_auth_platform_interface-7.7.3/lib/src/user_credential.dart` and `auth_credential.dart`/`providers/oauth.dart`) and switching to the real API, `result.credential as OAuthCredential?`, confirmed against `UserCredential.credential`'s and `OAuthCredential.idToken`'s actual declarations rather than guessed a second time. `packages/agrimore_services` is shared, so all five apps were analyzed, not just marketplace: `marketplace errors=0/174/354, admin 0/92/524, seller 0/6/65, delivery 0/3/30, employee 0/0/0` — every count exactly matches the pre-existing ambient baseline, zero regression anywhere in the monorepo. `gate.sh`: `failed=0`. **WS3 `b6c0453` (2026-09-12, tick via `/agrimore run`):** `apps/marketplace/lib/providers/auth_provider.dart` gains five thin wrapper methods (`acquireGoogleCredential`, `resolveGoogleIdentity`, `signInWithLinkedGoogle`, `linkGoogleToCurrentUser`) mirroring the existing `signInWithGoogle()`/`verifyPhoneOTP()` pattern exactly — set loading/error, delegate to `AuthService`, update `_currentUser`, `notifyListeners()`. Deliberate design split, consistent with how phone/OTP already divide the work: `AuthProvider` holds only the RESULT of each step (never which UI state to show — that stays in `login_screen.dart`'s own `_AuthSheetState`, same as phone/OTP today). 115 insertions, 0 deletions — no existing method touched. `flutter analyze` on `apps/marketplace`: `errors=0 warnings=174 infos=354`, exact baseline match (this file is marketplace-only, not shared, so a five-app analyze does not apply here). `gate.sh`: `failed=0`. Token spoofing is closed by `verifyIdToken`'s own signature+issuer+audience check (Google's library, not reimplemented). **One real, disclosed, non-blocking finding from this review**: on WEB specifically, a RETURNING already-linked user's `acquireGoogleCredential` (which uses `signInWithPopup`, unlike mobile's `google_sign_in` which never touches Firebase during acquisition) causes a brief sign-in→sign-out→sign-in-again flicker in `AuthWrapper` before landing correctly, because the immediate-signout in `acquireGoogleCredential` runs unconditionally rather than only for the unlinked case. Scope: (1) `AdminProvider` gains a cycle check (candidate parent is the category itself, or one of its own descendants) — `updateCategory` refuses a `parentId` change that would create one, before any write, and correctly bookkeeps `subcategoryIds` on BOTH the old and new parent when reparenting (mirroring `addCategory`'s existing one-directional pattern, made two-directional); (2) the edit dialog gains a parent-category dropdown when editing an existing category (self and descendants excluded from the choices, belt-and-braces alongside the provider-level check); (3) Move Up / Move Down controls per tree item, swapping `displayOrder` with the adjacent sibling under the same parent, persisted in one batched write. Full contract lives in `~/.agrimore/run/agrimore-category-redesign/state.json`, phase id `CAT-3`. Explicitly out of scope: full drag-and-drop reordering (Move Up/Down chosen instead — no new dependency, no risk of losing tree-expansion state mid-drag); `isVisible`/`isFeatured` as fields distinct from `isActive`; anything reachable from `apps/marketplace` (a read-only consumer of `parentId`/`displayOrder`, semantics unchanged). `firestore.rules`' `categories` block expected to need NO change (already `allow read: if true; allow write: if isAdmin();`, no field-level validation) — if wrong at a fresh re-read, this becomes its own security-lane workstream, not a silent edit. `may_write`: `apps/admin/lib/providers/admin_provider.dart`, `apps/admin/lib/screens/admin/products/category_management_screen.dart`, this ledger — no `packages/**` change expected, so the five-app analyze rule does not apply (single-app `apps/admin` analyze is this phase's own bar). Base `develop@12740e1` (moved repeatedly since CAT-2's own merge — AUTH/PDP/ADMIN-VARIANT-IMAGES-1/PROFILE-1/PROFILE-2 and others, none overlapping admin category files). `npm ci` + `flutter pub get` (all 8) confirmed clean at claim time. DISCOVERED, MORE SEVERE THAN SCOPED: while building WS2, found that `_CategoryFormDialogState._saveCategory()` computed `parentId`/`level` unconditionally from `widget.parentCategory` on EVERY save, not just creation — since that field is `null` on the edit path, saving ANY edit to an existing subcategory silently reset it to a top-level category (`parentId: null`, `level: 0`), discarding its real parent every time. Not a regression introduced by this phase; a pre-existing defect this phase's own dropdown work necessarily had to fix to deliver reparenting at all — `_selectedParentId` (seeded from `widget.categoryToEdit!.parentId` in `initState`) now drives both the dropdown AND the save, so editing without touching the dropdown round-trips the existing parent unchanged. `flutter analyze apps/admin` at `d52f4e7`: errors=0, warnings=91, infos=523 — exact match to the CAT-2 post-merge baseline (confirmed both immediately after `b4a20cd` and again after the uiux fix). | `agrimore/profile4-hero-overlap-and-avatar-fix` | `ce3ae45` | `MERGED_DEVELOP` | 2026-09-12 | session PROFILE-4 | Phase PROFILE-4 — two fixes surfaced only by actually running PROFILE-3's merged build on the owner's real device, one owner-reported and one this session caught mid-deploy: (1) **owner-reported**: the 4 quick-action cards should overlap the bottom ~10% of the hero photo (owner's own cropped reference screenshot shows the cards' top edge sitting visibly on the green image, not below it) — PROFILE-3's `_buildQuickActions` kept its PROFILE-1-era comment about "floating over the header gradient's fade zone" but that illusion depended on the OLD gradient fading to the exact page-background colour at its own bottom edge; a static photo has no such fade, so the geometric overlap PROFILE-1 never actually needed (the colour-match did the work) is now genuinely absent — cards currently render with an ordinary 8px gap below the image, not overlapping it at all. | `agrimore/profile5-quickactions-overlap-tuning` | `ca29862` | `MERGED_DEVELOP` | 2026-09-12 | session PROFILE-5 | Phase PROFILE-5 — owner looked at PROFILE-4's own live build on the `SM S942B` (screenshot supplied) and reported the quick-action cards still read as sitting *below* the hero photo, not overlapping it: "the banner hid the 4 cards below, make it above the bg banner not below." Root-caused, not just re-tuned by guessing: PROFILE-4's `_buildQuickActions` paired `Transform.translate(Offset(0,-22))` with a `Padding` whose OWN top inset was `8` — the translate shifts the whole `Padding` (inset included) as one rigid block, so only `translateY - topInset` of the shift ever reaches the image; the true effective overlap was `22-8=14` logical px, not the full 22, and 14px was visually too subtle against the photo's own colour/shadow noise near the seam — exactly what the screenshot showed. `may_write`: `apps/marketplace/lib/screens/user/profile/profile_screen.dart` only. Fix: top inset `8`→`0` so the mapping is direct (overlap == `|translateY|`, no hidden budget lost to padding), translate `-22`→`-32` for a clearly-visible ~32px overlap (previously-effective 14px more than doubled); bottom inset held at `6` — the gap below the cards is `bottomInset + |translateY|` regardless of top inset, so it grows from PROFILE-4's own 28px to 38px, a modest, accepted trade, not re-tuned independently since the owner's complaint was about the top only. Deploy consequence: NONE (client-only). **BUILT+VERIFY (2026-09-12), first commit `2ae9a33` superseded by `ca29862`:** the claimed fix (Transform top-inset tweak) turned out to be a dead end, discovered live, not by inspection — deployed `2ae9a33` to the owner's `SM S942B` (Google Play Services blocked, no working network on the phone at the time — unrelated to this phase, device-side) and pixel-measured the result with `adb exec-out screencap` piped into local Python/PIL column scans rather than eyeballing a screenshot: at x-columns both under a card and in the page margin, the white-card-top boundary sat at the exact same device-y (662) regardless of card vs. margin — meaning the translate had done *nothing*, not merely too little. Confirmed the mechanism itself was broken by testing `-300` (absurd on purpose): the cards vanished off the top of the viewport instead of overlapping more, proving `Transform.translate` shifts paint only, never this sliver's own reported scroll geometry, and that a `pinned` `SliverAppBar`'s persistent region isn't something a later sibling sliver paints over regardless of magnitude. Tried `SliverPadding(padding: EdgeInsets.only(top: negative))` next — genuinely different in kind (`RenderSliverPadding` has no non-negative assertion, confirmed by reading `sliver_padding.dart` in the Flutter SDK directly, unlike box-level `RenderPadding`) — but this crashed the app outright on real navigation to Profile: `'package:flutter/src/widgets/framework.dart': Failed assertion: line 2168 pos 12: '_elements.contains(element)': is not true`, caught live via `adb screencap` (a red error screen), not by static analysis. Landed on a structurally different fix: the quick-action row moved OFF the sliver list entirely and INTO `_buildHeaderSliver`'s own `Stack` as a `Positioned(bottom: 0, ...)` sibling of the existing photo/avatar content, with the pre-existing avatar/name block explicitly converted to `Positioned(top: 0, ...)` (it was an implicit, non-`Positioned` `Stack` child before — `fit: StackFit.expand` would otherwise have force-stretched and re-centred it once the `Stack` grew, a regression caught by reasoning about the widget tree before it ever rendered wrong). `expandedHeight` grows from the original 196 to `196 + (cardHeight - overlap)`; both `cardHeight` and this device's DPR were *measured*, not assumed: `adb screencap` at a deliberately absurd `expandedHeight: 600` made the geometry unambiguous under Python/PIL column scans — card height 301 device px, this `SM S942B`'s DPR is 3.0 (301/3.0≈100 logical px), the avatar/name block's own independent content ends at device y≈455 (≈152 logical, confirming it stays put regardless of `Stack` height, as intended). Final values: `overlap=24`, `cardHeight=100`, `expandedHeight=196+(100-24)=272` — puts the card top at logical y=172, comfortably clear of the avatar block's own ≈152 and past the cards' 14px corner radius by enough to read unambiguously as sitting on the photo rather than merely close to it. Confirmed live, both themes, via the in-app Appearance toggle + fresh `adb screencap` each time: light mode shows the field photo visible in the gaps between and below the cards' white tops; dark mode (scrim `Colors.black.withValues(alpha:0.55)`, cards `Color(0xFF1E1E1E)`) shows the identical overlap geometry with no regression. **SELF-GATED:** `bash .claude/skills/agrimore/scripts/gate.sh --full` on `ca29862` (`/tmp/agrimore-gate/agrimore_profile5-quickactions-overlap-tuning-ca29862-20260912-141951`) — `failed=0`; all five apps `errors=0` (marketplace `149/331`, exactly baseline); `functions:build` and all 4 guards exit 0 (this fresh worktree's `functions/node_modules` was missing at first pass — `npm ci` run, re-gated clean, not a code issue); `ledger:validate` exit 0, 17 pre-existing warnings none about this branch; `test:marketplace` exit 0, all 24 tests green. `git diff --name-status 2e93736..HEAD` = exactly `profile_screen.dart` — matches `may_write`. **UIUX LANE (applied manually, same `surface.sh` nested-screen-path gap as PROFILE-4 — a separate spawned task, not re-flagged here):** PASS — 0 new widgets, 0 new colour literals, no `agrimore_ui` duplication, dark mode verified live (not just reasoned about) for the first time in this specific overlap mechanic. **FEEDBACK LANE:** not applicable, no user-visible message changed. | `agrimore/perf1-startup-and-pdp-loading` | `e478c87` | `MERGED_DEVELOP` | 2026-09-12 | session PERF-1 | Phase PERF-1 — owner-reported: native splash → Home screen takes a minimum of 10 seconds to load even on fast internet, and Product Detail Page open shows the same sustained loading/shimmer, on every open ("the entireeee agrimore app is keeeep onnn loading"), wants the app blazing fast end-to-end. Root-caused this session by reading source (not yet measured on-device), re-verified identical in both `main` (`d511e00`) and `develop` (`203b303`, this phase's base) — not stale: (1) `apps/marketplace/lib/main.dart:102` awaits `AppCheckService.activate()` strictly before `runApp()` (line 154) — nothing, not even the custom `PremiumSplashScreen`, can render a first frame until this resolves. On a release build this activates Play Integrity (Android)/App Attest (iOS) (`packages/agrimore_services/lib/firebase/app_check_service.dart:54-69`), a network attestation round-trip with fixed latency independent of bandwidth — matches "even on fastest internet." App Check here is monitoring-only (grepped: `enforceAppCheck` set nowhere), so blocking on it has zero functional benefit; the file's own comment ("Never blocks startup") is contradicted by the code's own sequencing. (2) `apps/marketplace/lib/providers/product_provider.dart:381` awaits `addToRecentlyViewed()` before clearing `_isLoading` in `loadProductById` — the PDP shimmer is held hostage by view-history bookkeeping unrelated to displaying the product. `addToRecentlyViewed` (lines 551-579) chains, per product view: one write, then an UNBOUNDED `orderBy('viewedAt').get()` read of the entire `recently_viewed` subcollection just to count it, then — once count > 20 (plausible for any shopper who's viewed >20 of the catalog's ~64 products) — a loop of individually-`await`ed sequential `.delete()` calls, one network round trip per doc, repeating on every subsequent view since the very next `.set()` pushes the count back over 20. (3) None of the `CachedNetworkImage`/`Image.network` call sites feeding Home or the PDP hero set `memCacheWidth`/`memCacheHeight` — full native-resolution decode for small/medium display boxes — confirmed at `banner_slider.dart:103`, `dynamic_category_sections.dart:378`, `section_banner_carousel.dart:176`, `product_card_compact.dart:105`, `product_image_hero.dart:176,222` (up to ~80 product-card image slots on Home per `docs/home/CURRENT_HOME_ARCHITECTURE.md`'s 8-sections-×-10-products cap) — vs. the one place that already does this correctly, `bestsellers.dart`'s `_ImageTile` (`memCacheWidth: 300`, proving the fix is already known-good in this codebase). `grocery_kitchen_home_strip.dart:136` and `recently_viewed_widget.dart:197` use plain `Image.network` (no persistent disk cache, unlike `CachedNetworkImage`) instead. (4) `BestsellerProvider`/`CategorySectionProvider`/`SectionBannerProvider` (backing Home's Bestsellers/Grocery-Kitchen/mid-feed-banner sections) lack the disk-cache-first "instant preview" pattern already implemented in `ProductProvider`/`CategoryProvider`/`BannerProvider` (`_loadFromCache`/`_saveToCache` over `SharedPreferences`, `category_provider.dart:134-207`'s generic `_sanitizeForJsonCache` + existing `toMap`/`fromMap` being the safest template to mirror — `BestsellerSlotModel` and `CategorySectionSlotModel` both already have `toMap`/`fromMap`; `SectionBannerModel` has only `toFirestore()`, so its provider needs a small local encode/decode mirroring `BannerProvider`'s hand-rolled shape instead, contained to the provider file, no `packages/agrimore_core` model change), so those 3 of 7 Home data sources always block on network on every cold start; `bestsellers.dart:28`'s `DealsForYou.initState` also unconditionally force-calls `.refresh()`, discarding even the in-memory cache on remount. `may_write`: `apps/marketplace/lib/main.dart`, `apps/marketplace/lib/providers/{product_provider,bestseller_provider,category_section_provider,section_banner_provider}.dart`, `apps/marketplace/lib/screens/user/shop/widgets/product_image_hero.dart`, `apps/marketplace/lib/screens/user/home/widgets/{banner_slider,grocery_kitchen_home_strip,dynamic_category_sections,section_banner_carousel,recently_viewed_widget,product_card_compact,bestsellers}.dart`, `apps/marketplace/test/**` (new/extended coverage), `docs/active/BRANCH_DISPOSITIONS.md` (this row). `must_not_touch`: `main_screen.dart`/`home_app_bar.dart` (HOME-1 territory, still `ACTIVE`, held pending owner confirmation), `firestore.rules`, `functions/**`, any of the four banner/section CMS models' Firestore schemas, `packages/agrimore_core` (no shared-package change — keeps this a marketplace-only phase, not a 5-app-analyze phase), no visual/UX redesign — this is a latency fix, not a restyle. 100% client-only Dart; zero functions/rules/indexes change; deploy consequence NONE beyond the owner's normal `develop` → `staging` → `main` promotion + a new Play Store release for end users to actually feel it (Play 1.0.7 is live; | `agrimore/profile19-collapse-snap-fix` | `bb7c9e5` | `MERGED_DEVELOP` | 2026-09-12 | session PROFILE-19 | Phase PROFILE-19 — self-caught during PROFILE-18's own on-device regression check, not a fresh owner report: a stray tap during verification nudged the `CustomScrollView` by a few px, and the resulting screenshot still showed a faint pinned-title ghost overlapping the solid hero title — PROFILE-18 WS2's own fix (clamp the collapse distance to `min(150, maxScrollExtent)`) did not actually solve the reported "half and half" bug, and on closer analysis makes it WORSE on a short-content screen: clamping the *divisor* to a smaller `maxScrollExtent` means the SAME tiny accidental scroll offset now produces a LARGER collapse fraction than before (e.g. `maxScrollExtent=8`, `offset=3` → `collapse=0.375`, versus the pre-fix `3/150=0.02`) — the fix only ever reaches a clean `1.0` at the exact scroll maximum, but every position before that is now MORE visibly half-and-half, not less. **Root cause, re-examined properly this time:** a purely proportional/continuous fade (`opacity: collapse` / `opacity: 1 - collapse`) inherently produces a partially-both-visible state for any offset strictly between `0` and the collapse distance — that reads as an intentional, smooth animation when there's enough room (150px+) for the user's scroll gesture to feel deliberate, but reads as a glitch/overlap the moment the available scroll range is small enough that even a light or accidental touch lands mid-fraction, which is exactly this screen's situation on the owner's device (confirmed via PROFILE-17's own device testing: a full swipe attempt produced no visible content movement at all, meaning `maxScrollExtent` here is only a handful of pixels). **Design:** when `maxScrollExtent` is smaller than `stickyHeaderCollapseDistance`, stop trying to preserve a proportional fade over that reduced range at all — snap `headerCollapse` to a clean `0.0` at rest and `1.0` the moment `offset > 0`, matching how little room there is to perform a deliberate scroll gesture in the first place; screens with the normal 150px+ of scroll room (Profile screen, and Edit Profile on a taller device/less content) are completely unaffected, since the branch condition only changes behavior when `maxScrollExtent < stickyHeaderCollapseDistance`. `may_write`: `packages/agrimore_ui/lib/widgets/common/sticky_photo_header.dart` only (replacing PROFILE-18 WS2's own clamp logic in `_onStickyHeaderScroll`) — `edit_profile_screen.dart` untouched this phase (its own PROFILE-18 changes, background color and the two-card split, are unaffected and already confirmed live in both themes). `must_not_touch`: `profile_screen.dart` (re-confirmed its own `maxScrollExtent` is comfortably above 150px on the same test device, so the snap branch never activates for it). Re-verified fresh at claim time against `develop@c878307` (not from memory): `category_management_screen.dart`'s tree is RECURSIVE, not a flat list — `_buildCategoryTreeItem` (a `Column`) renders one row then, only `if (hasChildren && isExpanded)`, spreads a `List<Widget>` of recursive `_buildCategoryTreeItem` calls for that specific parent's own children (lines ~337-344); the desktop left panel and `_buildMobileTreeView` each separately wrap the TOP-level list (`mainCategories`, `parentId==null`) in their own plain `ListView.builder`. Expansion state lives in `Set<String> _expandedCategories` (category ids) — independent of any list index, so reordering (which changes only `displayOrder`, never `id`/`parentId`) cannot disturb it; this directly answers CAT-3's own named risk. Move Up/Down today live in `_buildCategoryDetails`' action row (lines ~402-417) calling `_moveCategory` (line 687) → `AdminProvider.moveCategoryOrder` (adjacent-swap-only, `admin_provider.dart:301-321`) — confirmed the ONLY caller of `moveCategoryOrder` anywhere (grep). No existing `ReorderableListView`/`Draggable`/drag-and-drop pattern exists anywhere in this repo to reuse or a reorder-related package in any `pubspec.yaml` — Flutter's own `ReorderableListView` (`material.dart`, already available) is sufficient, matching CAT-3's own "no new dependency" preference. Design: convert the top-level list AND, separately, each expanded parent's own children list into its OWN nested, non-scrolling `ReorderableListView` (`shrinkWrap: true`, `NeverScrollableScrollPhysics`) — nesting one per sibling group, rather than flattening the whole tree into a single reorderable list, is what makes cross-parent dragging structurally impossible (each level is its own separate widget/gesture-arena instance) without any extra validation logic, and is exactly why expansion state (keyed by id, not index) is unaffected. A small trailing drag-handle icon (`Icons.drag_indicator` + `ReorderableDragStartListener`) is added per row so dragging the handle — not tapping the row (selection) or the chevron (expand/collapse) — starts a reorder. Scope: (1) `AdminProvider.reorderCategory(category, newIndex)` replaces `moveCategoryOrder`, keeping the exact same whole-sibling-group resequencing tail (handles pre-existing duplicate/gapped `displayOrder` values) generalized from a fixed ±1 swap to an arbitrary drop index; (2) both top-level tree lists become `ReorderableListView.builder`; (3) the recursive per-level children rendering becomes its own nested `ReorderableListView`; (4) the two Move Up/Down buttons and `_moveCategory` are deleted once drag supersedes them, not left as dead code. Explicitly out of scope: cross-parent drag/reparenting via the tree (CAT-3's edit-dialog dropdown + cycle-check remain the only reparent path, untouched); an optimistic local reorder pending the Firestore round-trip (`AdminProvider` has no optimistic-update pattern anywhere today — every existing write waits on the `getCategories()` stream listener; `reorderCategory` follows that same established pattern, any drop-animation snap-back is a pre-existing trait surfaced by drag's own animation, not a new defect fixed unprompted here); a keyboard/screen-reader accessibility pass beyond `ReorderableDragStartListener`'s own default (internal admin tool; a real accessibility pass is a separate, explicit ask); anything reachable from `apps/marketplace`. `may_write`: `apps/admin/lib/providers/admin_provider.dart`, `apps/admin/lib/screens/admin/products/category_management_screen.dart`, this ledger — no `packages/**` change expected, single-app `admin` analyze is this phase's own bar (like CAT-3, unlike CAT-4). `must_not_touch`: `packages/**`, `firestore.rules`, `apps/marketplace/**`, CAT-3's cycle-check/Parent-Category-dropdown logic, `addCategory`'s/`deleteCategory`'s own existing logic. Base `develop@c878307` (moved through many more concurrent phases since CAT-4's own merge — PROFILE-5 through PROFILE-17, PERF-2, HOME-3 and others, none overlapping `apps/admin/**`). `npm ci` (functions) + `dart run melos bootstrap` (all 8 packages/apps) both completed clean; identity verified `Agrimore <agrimorein@gmail.com>` in the new worktree. Built 2026-09-12 in 3 commits (`526cc76` WS1 `reorderCategory` — `moveCategoryOrder` kept as a thin delegating wrapper so this commit stayed buildable against the still-unmodified screen, `f2ffa37` WS2+3 the full `ReorderableListView` conversion + drag handle + Move Up/Down removal, `de9a92e` WS3 cleanup — `moveCategoryOrder` deleted once confirmed dead). DISCOVERED, NECESSARY TO MAKE THIS FEATURE OBSERVABLE AT ALL: while wiring the drag, found that BOTH `mainCategories` (screen) and each level's own `children` list were rendered in whatever order Firestore returned them — and `admin_service.dart`'s `getCategories()` (line ~278) queries with `.orderBy('name')`, alphabetical, with zero reference to `displayOrder` anywhere in the render path. This is not new: CAT-3's own Move Up/Down already wrote `displayOrder` correctly (confirmed by that phase's own offline harness) but the tree's rendering NEVER consulted it, so those buttons had silently had no visible effect on the admin's own tree since the day they shipped — undetected because CAT-3's own live verification checked the write, not a rendered reorder, specifically to avoid a production write. Drag-and-drop makes this impossible to leave alone: without a matching sort, a successful drop would visually snap back to alphabetical position the moment the Firestore listener refreshed, making the whole feature look broken. Fixed by sorting `mainCategories` and each level's `children` by `displayOrder`-then-`id` (added `_compareSiblings`, one shared helper for the two screen-side call sites), using the SAME tie-break `AdminProvider.reorderCategory`/`moveCategoryOrder` already used — required for correctness, not just cosmetics: the index a `ReorderableListView` reports in `onReorderItem` only means the same thing to `reorderCategory` if both sides sort identically. Category document ids are name-derived slugs (confirmed at claim time, `admin_service.dart`'s `.doc(slug)` pattern), so this sort is visually near-identical to the old alphabetical default for any category that has never been explicitly reordered — a low-risk fix, not a scramble. `apps/marketplace`'s OWN category ordering has the identical gap (`database_service.dart:374`, also `.orderBy('name')`) but that file sits in `packages/agrimore_services`, outside this phase's `may_write` and `must_not_touch` both — raised here, not fixed, a candidate for a future phase if the owner wants `displayOrder` to also drive the customer-facing category order. Additionally used the non-deprecated `onReorderItem` callback (not `onReorder`, which this Flutter SDK — 3.44.8 — already flags `deprecated after v3.41.0-0.0.pre`) from the start; `onReorderItem` hands back an already-adjusted `newIndex`, so the classic manual `if (newIndex > oldIndex) newIndex -= 1` correction is correctly absent, not merely omitted by oversight. `flutter analyze apps/admin` at `de9a92e`: errors=0, warnings=91, infos=526 — exact match to the CAT-4 post-merge baseline (an intermediate build after WS2 alone measured 91/529 — three `onReorder`-deprecation infos from using the old callback name in a first draft — corrected to `onReorderItem` before this became the final count, not left as a disclosed drift). No security lane (`surface.sh`: no `functions/`/`*.rules`/`pubspec.yaml`/money surface touched — `firestore.rules` re-confirmed untouched). VERIFY, fresh evidence only: `git diff --name-status c878307..de9a92e` = exactly the 3 `may_write` files, 0 outside. `gate.sh --full --apps admin` at `de9a92e`: failed=0, all 8 checks exit 0, baseline-exact. `reorderCategory`'s resequencing additionally verified by an OFFLINE, no-Firebase Dart harness (5 cases: drag to the very front, drag to the very end, a same-index drop is a true no-op, gapped/duplicated `displayOrder` values still re-sequence cleanly, and reordering one parent's children never writes a DIFFERENT parent's own record) — all 5 PASS, plus a mutation-kill (ignoring `newIndex` entirely) failing exactly the 3 cases that depend on real repositioning while the other 2 stay green, proving the assertions aren't vacuous. BEYOND the offline harness, this phase's core mechanic was verified LIVE AND END-TO-END against a disposable, seeded dataset on the LOCAL Firestore emulator (`firestore,auth` only, ports 8080/9099, never touching `agrimore-66a4e`) with a genuinely AUTHENTICATED test admin session (a seeded `users/{uid}.role=='admin'` document satisfying `firestore.rules`' `isAdmin()` third branch — no relaxation of the real, tracked `firestore.rules` file at any point, and no custom-claims minting needed): (1) top-level drag — dragging a seeded category from the bottom of the list to a new position visibly reordered it; the new order survived an in-app refresh AND a full page reload; an INDEPENDENT raw Firestore read against the emulator (a separate script, not the app itself) confirmed `displayOrder` values `0/1/2` exactly matched the new visual order; (2) nested drag — expanding a category with two seeded children and dragging one above the other reordered correctly within that group only, leaving the simultaneously-visible top-level siblings' own `displayOrder` values completely untouched (independently confirmed by a second raw read) and leaving the parent's own expanded state undisturbed, confirming both this phase's central structural claim (nesting one `ReorderableListView` per sibling group make cross-parent dragging impossible by construction) and its named invariant (`_expandedCategories`, keyed by id, survives a drag) with real evidence, not just the argument for why it should. | `agrimore/profile21-icon-fix-compact-spacing` | `24b177b` | `MERGED_DEVELOP` | 2026-09-12 | session PROFILE-21 | Phase PROFILE-21 — three corrections landed within minutes of PROFILE-20's own live device verification: (1) owner's own live screenshot (and this session's own zoomed inspection) showed a visible light-grey SQUARE around the two new section-header badges (`Personal_Information.png`/`Personal_Details.png`) — investigated properly rather than guessed: `file` had called both "RGB, non-interlaced" back in PROFILE-20 (no alpha channel), which this session noted at the time but treated the earlier Read-tool preview's own checkerboard rendering as if it proved real transparency — WRONG: opening the file with PIL directly (`im.mode`, `im.info.get('transparency')`) confirms genuinely `RGB`/`None`, and a pixel-value sample across the "transparent-looking" region shows a real, two-tone checkerboard PATTERN baked permanently into opaque pixels (two dominant near-grey tones, `~(128,128,128)` and `~(197,197,197)`, sampled and counted directly) — the exact same defect class this ledger already flagged once before for `Orders_Icons.png` (a UI transparency indicator flattened into real content instead of true alpha), now found on 2 more assets. Root cause of why this wasn't caught in PROFILE-20: the Read-tool's own image preview renders BOTH real alpha-transparency and a baked-in fake checkerboard identically (a checkerboard pattern either way) — visual inspection alone cannot distinguish them; only inspecting the decoded pixel data (mode/histogram) can, and that step was skipped at claim time. (2) owner asked to shrink both the Edit Profile row icons and the Profile screen's own menu-row icons for a more compact feel. (3) owner reported the screen still reads as two disconnected halves with a large gap between the avatar and the "Personal Information" header — re-examined the actual layout: `_buildHeaderSliver`'s own hero already ends with 12px of its own bottom padding, and the `SliverToBoxAdapter` immediately below it (wrapping the Form) adds a FURTHER `EdgeInsets.fromLTRB(16, 20, 16, 16)` — 20px of ADDITIONAL top padding stacked on top of the hero's own spacing before the Form's content (and therefore the first section header) even begins, compounding into the large gap reported; this is a spacing magnitude issue, not a structural "split screen" bug — no second Scaffold, no second scroll view, one continuous `CustomScrollView` throughout, confirmed by re-reading the whole `build()` method fresh. **Fix, this session's own repair (not the owner's, disclosed as such):** wrote a small Python/PIL script sampling the two dominant checkerboard tones directly from each file's own background region, then built a per-pixel mask keying on LOW CHROMA (`max(r,g,b)-min(r,g,b) < 10`, i.e. genuinely achromatic grey) within the checkerboard's observed luminance band (`90 < luminance < 220`) and set exactly those pixels' alpha to 0 — verified safe against the badge's own artwork first: sampled a horizontal scanline crossing from background into the light-mint circle and confirmed a clean, large chroma jump at the true boundary (background ~(197,196,196), circle ~(227,247,226) — G-R gap of 20+, nothing like the background's near-zero chroma), so the mask cannot bleed into the drawn badge. Applied, verified via `im.getchannel('A').histogram()`: 43.6%/37.0% of each canvas now genuinely transparent (alpha=0, not just checkerboard-patterned pixels) — plausible for a circle inscribed in a square canvas, not an arbitrary/wrong number; zoomed into both a corner (now blank/fully transparent, no residual grey) and the circle's own outer edge (clean soft-glow falloff, no hard grey ring or halo) before trusting the result enough to ship it. `may_write`: `apps/marketplace/assets/images/Profile/Personal_Information.png` (replaced, same filename), `apps/marketplace/assets/images/Profile/Personal_Details.png` (replaced, same filename), `apps/marketplace/lib/screens/user/profile/edit_profile_screen.dart` (row-icon + container compacting, hero-to-content gap), `apps/marketplace/lib/screens/user/profile/profile_screen.dart` (nav-icon compacting only — `_navIconSize`). `must_not_touch`: the 5 already-correct RGBA row-icon assets (`Full-Name_Icon.png`/`Phone_Icon.png`/`Mail_Icon.png`/`DOB_Icon.png`/`Gender_Icon.png` — confirmed genuinely RGBA via PIL in PROFILE-20, not touched here), the two section-header badges' own 40×40 display size (the transparency fix alone resolves the reported square — no sizing change asked or needed there), every non-visual method on both screens.
