Static-code-reading diagnosis had now under-delivered twice, so this phase got REAL evidence first: built+installed the current `develop` tip fresh onto the connected physical device (`RZGL21W98TB`, `SM S942B` — the same shared device other concurrent phases use for their own device verification; confirmed via `adb devices`/`dumpsys activity` it was momentarily foregrounded before use, force-stopped cleanly, no other session's flow was mid-transaction), captured `adb logcat -v time` across a real cold start with an already-persisted login (owner's own real session, not a fresh OTP — no OTP spent), and read the ACTUAL timestamps against the app's own existing debugPrint markers rather than assuming from source. Verified findings, all first-hand from that capture: process launch (`ActivityTaskManager START`) at 21:35:47.399; Firebase initialized 48.225; `AuthWrapper`'s own `getUserData` (PERF-2's now-parallel two reads) resolved in ~1.46s (48.434→49.897) — real, not the dominant cost. Then, at 50.191 — only 294ms after Home's own content began loading — a SECOND, entirely redundant `🔥 Getting user data for` fired, preceded by `🔄 Refreshing user data...`, i.e. `AuthProvider.refreshUserData()` (`auth_provider.dart:1081`) — its only apps/marketplace caller is `profile_screen.dart:118`'s `_checkAuthAndLoadData()`, called from `ProfileScreen.initState()`. The owner never tapped Profile. Root cause, confirmed by reading `main_screen.dart`: `_buildScreens()` (its `IndexedStack` children list, `main_screen.dart:65-82`) unconditionally constructs ALL FIVE tab screens — `HomeScreen`, `ShopScreen`, `CategoriesScreen`, `CartScreen`, `ProfileScreen` — on `MainScreen`'s very first build, regardless of which tab is actually selected; `IndexedStack` only controls which child is *painted*, not which are *constructed* — so all five `initState()`s fire together, right at the moment Home's own critical-path loading is happening. The captured log directly shows the collision: `📦 Products loading in progress, waiting...` and `📂 Categories loading in progress, waiting...` each appear MULTIPLE times in the same ~1ms window (50.190-50.191) — proof that more than one of the five simultaneously-constructed tabs independently called `ProductProvider.loadProducts()`/`CategoryProvider.loadCategories()` and hit the race-guard, not just Home's own single call. `ProfileScreen.initState()` alone adds three concurrent, unnecessary Firestore/provider operations at the exact moment Home is trying to become interactive (`refreshUserData()`, `SellerProvider.checkSellerStatus()`, `WalletProvider.loadWallet()`); Shop/Categories/Cart screens were not individually audited for their own `initState` cost in this phase (out of scope — the fix below removes ALL of it uniformly, not screen-by-screen) but the repeated race-guard log lines confirm at least one of them is also independently re-requesting products/categories. This is the same class of file (`main_screen.dart`) HOME-1 already redesigned the bottom-nav chrome of (merged, no conflict — confirmed HOME-1's own commit `8d816c1` is the most recent prior touch, already an ancestor of this phase's base, and this phase's `_buildScreens()`/`initState` edits are structurally disjoint from HOME-1's nav-chrome-only diff). Fix: tabs are now built lazily — a `_builtTabs` set (seeded with `widget.initialIndex`) gates each non-Home screen's real widget vs. a `SizedBox.shrink()` placeholder in `_buildScreens()`; `_onTabTapped` and the pre-existing `_onShopEntryChanged` (the second, non-tap place `_currentIndex` changes, for the `/category/:id` deep-link → Shop-tab flow) both add to `_builtTabs` alongside their existing `_currentIndex` update, so a tab's real screen is constructed once, the first time it is actually selected, and `IndexedStack` preserves its State on every rebuild after that exactly as it already does for the always-built Home. | `agrimore/cat7-category-drag-drop-reparent` | `89e14e7` | `MERGED_DEVELOP` | 2026-09-12 | session CAT-7 (run-state at `~/.agrimore/run/agrimore-category-redesign/state.json`, depends_on CAT-3 `MERGED_DEVELOP`, CAT-5 `MERGED_DEVELOP`) | Phase CAT-7 — owner asked to build the capability CAT-5's own row explicitly listed as out of scope: "cross-parent drag/reparenting via the tree (CAT-3's edit-dialog dropdown + cycle-check remain the only reparent path, untouched)". Re-verified fresh at claim time against `develop@0963211` (not from memory): CAT-5's nested-`ReorderableListView`-per-sibling-group design (one `ReorderableListView` instance per expanded parent, `category_management_screen.dart:361-384`) makes cross-parent drops structurally impossible by construction — a drag can never leave its own gesture-arena/list instance — so this phase cannot extend that mechanism; it adds a second, independent drag system alongside it. Design: each tree row's existing `ReorderableDragStartListener`-wrapped `drag_indicator_rounded` icon (same-level reorder, CAT-5, untouched) gets a SECOND, visually distinct icon (`Icons.open_with_rounded`, wrapped in a `Tooltip`) that starts a `LongPressDraggable<CategoryModel>` — a separate hit-test region, not an overlapping gesture recognizer, so the two drag systems cannot contend for the same pointer. Every tree row becomes a `DragTarget<CategoryModel>` (highights on hover); the tree header becomes a second `DragTarget<CategoryModel>` accepting a drop to reparent to top-level (`parentId: null`). Both targets reuse `AdminProvider`'s existing cycle-check and reparent-with-bookkeeping logic (`updateCategory`/`_wouldCreateCategoryCycle`, CAT-3) rather than duplicating it — `_wouldCreateCategoryCycle` is file-private, so `AdminProvider` gains one new PUBLIC passthrough method (`wouldCreateCategoryCycle`) for the screen's `onWillAcceptWithDetails` to call for live hover feedback; the actual write still goes through the same `updateCategory` both this phase and CAT-3's own dialog call. TWO adjacent, pre-existing gaps in `updateCategory`'s reparent path were found while reading it for reuse, both judged in-scope because drag-and-drop makes reparenting a subtree with descendants a common, low-friction action for the first time (previously only reachable through the buried edit-dialog dropdown): (1) `updateCategory` recomputes nothing about `level` for the moved category's DESCENDANTS — the dialog's own `_saveCategory` (`category_management_screen.dart:1312`) already recomputes the moved category's OWN `level` as `resolvedParent.level + 1` before calling `updateCategory`, but neither call site has ever touched any descendant's `level`, so reparenting a category with children already silently leaves every descendant's `level` stale (wrong icon color/label) — pre-existing since CAT-3, not previously visible because the dropdown made moving a subtree rare. Fix: `updateCategory` gains a recursive descendant-`level` cascade, applied only `if isReparent`, computed as a single delta (`category.level` minus the pre-move stored level) and applied uniformly to every transitive descendant via the existing `_categoryModels`/`parentId` tree — fixes both this phase's new drag path AND the pre-existing dialog path in one place, no duplication. (2) Neither call site checks whether moving a category WITH descendants would push any of them past the model's own documented 4-level cap (`CategoryModel.level` doc: "0=Main, 1=Sub, 2=Sub-Sub, 3=Sub-Sub-Sub"; `canHaveChildren => level < 3`) — the existing `_validParentChoices()` filters candidate parents by `canHaveChildren`, which bounds the MOVED category's own new level, but never accounts for that category's own subtree height, so moving a non-leaf category onto a level-2 or level-3 parent can silently push its grandchildren past level 3 today, via the existing dialog, unnoticed because it's rare enough to not yet have been hit. Fix: a subtree-height guard (`target.level + 1 + subtreeHeight(dragged) > 3` → reject) added to this phase's own `DragTarget.onWillAcceptWithDetails`/`onAcceptWithDetails` for the new drag path specifically — NOT retrofitted onto the pre-existing dialog dropdown, which is `must_not_touch` this phase (disclosed as a found-but-deferred gap in the dialog path, not silently left unmentioned). `may_write`: `apps/admin/lib/providers/admin_provider.dart` (the `wouldCreateCategoryCycle` passthrough + `updateCategory`'s descendant-level cascade only — `_wouldCreateCategoryCycle`'s own cycle-walk logic, `addCategory`, `reorderCategory` untouched), `apps/admin/lib/screens/admin/products/category_management_screen.dart` (new Draggable/DragTarget wiring at both tree-row call sites and the header; new `_draggingCategory` state field for the header's hover hint), this ledger. `must_not_touch`: `packages/**` (no model change — `parentId`/`level` already exist), `firestore.rules`/`storage.rules`/`functions/**` (same `categories` collection, same field shapes, `allow write: if isAdmin()` re-confirmed unchanged at `firestore.rules:649-661`, no field-level validation to satisfy), CAT-5's own same-level `ReorderableListView`/`reorderCategory` mechanism, CAT-3's own edit-dialog Parent Category dropdown and its `_validParentChoices`/`_descendantsOf` (reused for cycle logic, not rewritten), any `apps/marketplace` file. DISCOVERED, FIXED LIVE DURING THE UIUX LANE (not this phase's own new logic, but the exact reparent-write path this phase elevates to a first-class interaction): `CategoryModel.copyWith`'s `parentId: parentId ?? this.parentId` treats an explicit `copyWith(parentId: null)` identically to omitting the argument — the drag-to-header (reparent to top level) path is the ONLY caller anywhere in the repository that has ever passed an explicit null there (grepped, confirmed), so this pre-existing gap had never been hit before. First symptom: dragging a category onto the tree header visibly did nothing, but a raw Firestore read showed `level` had updated while `parentId` silently had not. Fixed with a sentinel-default pattern (`static const _unset = Object()`, `Object? parentId = _unset`, `identical(parentId, _unset) ? this.parentId : parentId as String?`) so omitting the parameter still preserves the existing value (every other call site, none of which ever passes `parentId`, is unaffected) while an explicit null now takes effect; re-verified live after a rebuild — confirmed correct via the rendered tree (category now shown at top level) and an independent raw Firestore read (both `parentId` and the old parent's `subcategoryIds` update correctly). `flutter analyze` all five apps at `89e14e7`: errors=0 everywhere; marketplace 145/330, admin 91/531 (+2 infos vs the CAT-6/HOME-5 baseline of 529 — the two new hover-highlight `accentColor.withOpacity(...)` calls this phase adds, same already-pervasive `deprecated_member_use` class this file already carries six other instances of, not a new lint), seller 6/65, delivery 3/30, employee 0/0 — warnings baseline-exact in all five, the only drift is the disclosed +2 admin infos. On any install whose category order/names don't happen to match this exact assumed sequence, a chunk of e.g. Electronics/Toys/Books categories would still be headlined "Grocery & Kitchen" — a real, user-visible, hardcoded-and-false-content bug (`_FallbackCategorySection`'s own `title` renders as a prominent 16px/w700 section header, confirmed by reading it), the same class of issue this whole Home-CMS programme exists to remove. Design: remove the position-indexed `fallbackTitles` list and its lookup; every fallback section gets the same honest, generic title ("More Categories") already used today for any chunk beyond the fixed list's own length — extending the ALREADY-EXISTING honest default to every chunk, not inventing new copy. Design: `_validParentChoices()`'s own `c.canHaveChildren` term is replaced with `c.level + 1 + ownSubtreeHeight <= 3`, where `ownSubtreeHeight` is `AdminProvider.subtreeHeightOf(editingId)` (CAT-7's own already-public, already-tested method, reused rather than re-implemented locally — the dialog already calls into `AdminProvider` inline via `Provider.of<AdminProvider>(context, listen: false)` at nine other call sites in this same file, so this introduces no new pattern). | `agrimore/fix-state1-status-stop-render` | `7138519` | `MERGED_DEVELOP` | 2026-09-13 | session FIX-STATE-1 (run-state at `~/.agrimore/run/agrimore/state.json`) | Phase FIX-STATE-1 — user reported a reproducible bug in this repo's own tracked skill tooling, `.claude/skills/agrimore/scripts/state.sh`, discovered incidentally while closing out the HOME-9 phase (its own `status` subcommand rendered `stop: undefined (function at() { [native code] })` in `STATUS.md` instead of the actual stop reason). Root-caused, not assumed from the report alone: the dedicated `state.sh stop <programme> <reason>` subcommand (line 52) stores `s.stop` as an object, `{ at: <iso-timestamp>, reason: <string> }` — but both rendering call sites (`show` at line 38, `status` at line 57) unconditionally read `s.stop.reason` (and `status` additionally reads `s.stop.at`), assuming that object shape without checking it. The `agrimore-home-redesign` programme's own `stop` field had instead been set via the generic `state.sh set <programme> stop '"<a plain JSON string>"'` command (a real, easy-to-make caller mistake this bug report itself demonstrates — nothing in the tool's own usage text warns that `stop` has a special shape distinct from every other field `set` can write) — producing a bare STRING, not the `{at,reason}` object. Reading a plain string's `.reason` property returns `undefined`; reading a plain string's `.at` property (without calling it) returns the inherited `String.prototype.at` method itself, whose default string coercion is exactly `function at() { [native code] }` — this explains the exact garbled text observed, not a generic "undefined" or a crash. Confirmed this is NOT the `agrimore-home-redesign` programme's own fault to fix — the standalone `agrimore` programme's own pre-existing `stop` field (set correctly, historically, via the dedicated subcommand) renders perfectly fine today, proving the object-shape path already works; only the string-shape path (reachable via the generic, deliberately-unconstrained `set` command, a legitimate way to write any field) was unhandled. Design: add one small helper, `stopText(stop)`, used by both `show` and `status`, that renders `null`/`undefined` as no-stop, a well-formed `{reason, at}` object as `reason (at)` (byte-identical to today's existing correct output), a plain string as itself with no `(at)` suffix (no timestamp exists in that shape), and anything else via a safe `String(...)` fallback rather than crashing or producing token-garbage. Deliberately NOT adding validation/rejection to the generic `set` command for the `stop` field specifically — the user's own report asked for tolerant rendering, not a new constraint on an intentionally general-purpose command; `set` stays usable for any field, unconstrained, as it already is for every other field in this schema. `may_write`: `.claude/skills/agrimore/scripts/state.sh`, this ledger. `must_not_touch`: every other script in `.claude/skills/agrimore/scripts/`, every existing `~/.agrimore/run/*/state.json` file (outside the repository entirely, not touched or migrated — the rendering fix alone makes both the historical string-shaped and future object-shaped `stop` values display correctly, no data migration needed), every app/package/function file (this is pure shared-tooling maintenance, zero application surface touched). Lanes: none expected (no rules/functions/auth/payment/UI file touched) — to confirm via `surface.sh` on the actual diff. Every category in this database has therefore always shown `0 products` (or whatever stale value it was seeded with) regardless of how many real products are actually assigned to it — a materially different, more actionable gap than CAT-6's own `subcategoryIds` finding ("a write-only field, harmless in practice... zero readers"): THIS field IS read and IS shown to a human as if authoritative, so it actively misinforms rather than sitting inert. A SEPARATE legacy-data fallback exists for when a product's `categoryId`/`categoryName` holds a NAME instead of a real id — confirmed genuinely possible, not hypothetical, by reading `ProductModel.parseCategoryId`'s own fallback chain (`packages/agrimore_core/lib/models/product_model.dart:257-272`): `map['categoryId'] -> map['category']['id'] -> map['category'] as String -> map['categoryName'] -> 'general'` — legacy/RN-era documents can and provably do carry a name-shaped value in this field. But that fallback ONLY compares the product's name/slug against the SINGLE top-level `category` argument's own name/slug — never against any of its descendants' names/slugs, even though the ID-based check immediately above it already correctly covers the whole descendant set. Concrete, reproducible asymmetry (independent of what real production data happens to contain): a legacy product whose `categoryId` holds "Apples" (a subcategory's NAME, no real id) is invisible when browsing the PARENT "Fruits" category, while an otherwise-identical modern product with a real `categoryId: "cat_apples"` IS found correctly — the two paths answer the same membership question differently for equivalent products, silently hiding some legacy-tagged products from their own parent category on all 12 call sites at once. Design: extend the name/slug fallback to check every category in the SAME descendant set the id-based check already computes, instead of only the one top-level argument — a strict widening that can only ADD true results the existing code already misses, never remove or change a result for any case that already matches today (the top-level category is always included first, so the no-descendants case is byte-identical to current behaviour). ## Promotions

Fast-forward promotions between the three environment branches (`develop` → `staging` → `main`),
one row per promotion, newest last. The SHA promoted is the SHA that was tested on the source
branch; a promotion that is not a fast-forward is refused. Pushes happen only with the owner's
word in the same message, quoted here. There is no CI and no staging Firebase project, so the
last column records the local emulator sweep and the deploy command handed to the owner — never
a deploy the skill performed.
